.

Proof of Lemma
C.3
.
By definition of the GMM
criterion,

J_{T}(\theta;A_{1}C_{1},W_{1})=\bar{g}_{0,T}(\theta)^{\top}(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes A_{1}C_{1})((\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes C_{1}^{\top}A_{1}^{\top})W_{1}(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes A_{1}C_{1}))^{-1}(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes C_{1}^{\top}A_{1}^{\top})\bar{g}_{0,T}(\theta)

(C.23)

where

\bar{g}_{0,T}(\theta)\equiv\begin{pmatrix}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{t}(y_{e,t}-\phi p_{t})\\
T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{t}(p_{t}-\psi y_{S,t})\end{pmatrix}.

Using the identity

(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes C_{1}^{\top}A_{1}^{\top})=(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes C_{1}^{\top})(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes A_{1}^{\top})\ \text{and }(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes A_{1}C_{1})=(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes A_{1})(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes C_{1}),

we obtain

((\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes C_{1}^{\top}A_{1}^{\top})W_{1}(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes A_{1}C_{1}))^{-1}=(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes C_{1})^{-1}((\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes A_{1}^{\top})W_{1}(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes A_{1}))^{-1}(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes C_{1}^{\top})^{-1}.

(C.24)

Substituting (
C.24
) into (
C.23
), we get

\displaystyle J_{T}(\theta;A_{1}C_{1},W_{1})

\displaystyle=\bar{g}_{0,T}(\theta)^{\top}(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes A_{1})(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes C_{1})

\displaystyle\times(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes C_{1})^{-1}((\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes A_{1}^{\top})W_{1}(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes A_{1}))^{-1}(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes C_{1}^{\top})^{-1}

\displaystyle\times(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes C_{1}^{\top})(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes A_{1}^{\top})\bar{g}_{0,T}(\theta)

\displaystyle=\bar{g}_{0,T}(\theta)^{\top}(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes A_{1})((\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes A_{1}^{\top})W_{1}(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes A_{1}))^{-1}(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes A_{1}^{\top})\bar{g}_{0,T}(\theta)

\displaystyle=J_{T}(\theta;A_{1},W_{1}),

which establishes the claim of the lemma.
Q.E.D.

Lemma C.4
.

Under Assumptions
2
(i, ii) and
3
, we
have:

T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-{\mathbb{E}[}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}])=O_{p}(1)\text{ \ and \ }T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}(p_{t}y_{t}-{\mathbb{E}[}p_{t}y_{t}])=O_{p}(1).

Proof of Lemma
C.4
.
From (
2
) and
(
19
), we obtain

\displaystyle y_{t}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbf{I}_{n}+\frac{\phi\psi}{1-\phi\psi}\mathbf{1}_{n}S_{t}^{\top}\right)(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})+\frac{\phi}{1-\phi\psi}\mathbf{1}_{n}\varepsilon_{t},

(C.25)

\displaystyle p_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{\psi}{1-\phi\psi}S_{t}^{\top}(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})+\frac{\varepsilon_{t}}{1-\phi\psi}.

(C.26)

Therefore,

\displaystyle T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}

\displaystyle=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\left(\mathbf{I}_{n}+\frac{\phi\psi}{1-\phi\psi}\mathbf{1}_{n}S_{t}^{\top}\right)(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})^{\top}\left(\mathbf{I}_{n}+\frac{\phi\psi}{1-\phi\psi}S_{t}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\phi}{1-\phi\psi}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\left(\mathbf{I}_{n}+\frac{\phi\psi}{1-\phi\psi}\mathbf{1}_{n}S_{t}^{\top}\right)(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})\varepsilon_{t}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\phi}{1-\phi\psi}\mathbf{1}_{n}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\varepsilon_{t}(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})^{\top}\left(\mathbf{I}_{n}+\frac{\phi\psi}{1-\phi\psi}S_{t}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\phi^{2}}{(1-\phi\psi)^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{n}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\varepsilon_{t}^{2},

(C.27)

and

\displaystyle T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}p_{t}y_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{\psi}{1-\phi\psi}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\left(\mathbf{I}_{n}+\frac{\phi\psi}{1-\phi\psi}\mathbf{1}_{n}S_{t}^{\top}\right)(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})^{\top}S_{t}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{1-\phi\psi}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\left(\mathbf{I}_{n}+\frac{\phi\psi}{1-\phi\psi}\mathbf{1}_{n}S_{t}^{\top}\right)(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})\varepsilon_{t}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\phi\psi}{(1-\phi\psi)^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{n}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\varepsilon_{t}(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})^{\top}S_{t}+\frac{\phi}{(1-\phi\psi)^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{n}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\varepsilon_{t}^{2}.

(C.28)

From Assumptions
2
(i, ii), we have

\displaystyle T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})^{\top}

\displaystyle=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}{\mathbb{E}[}(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})^{\top}]+O_{p}(T^{-1/2}),

(C.29)

\displaystyle T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})\varepsilon_{t}

\displaystyle=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}{\mathbb{E}[}(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})\varepsilon_{t}]+O_{p}(T^{-1/2}),

(C.30)

\displaystyle T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\varepsilon_{t}^{2}

\displaystyle=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}{\mathbb{E}[}\varepsilon_{t}^{2}]+O_{p}(T^{-1/2}).

(C.31)

Similarly, from Assumptions
2
(i) and
3
(i), it follows
that

\displaystyle T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}S_{t}^{\top}(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})^{\top}S_{t}

\displaystyle=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}{\mathbb{E}[}S_{t}^{\top}(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})^{\top}S_{t}]+O_{p}(T^{-1/2}),

(C.32)

\displaystyle T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}S_{t}^{\top}(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})^{\top}

\displaystyle=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}{\mathbb{E}[}S_{t}^{\top}(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})^{\top}]+O_{p}(T^{-1/2}),

(C.33)

\displaystyle T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}S_{t}^{\top}(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})\varepsilon_{t}

\displaystyle=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}{\mathbb{E}[}S_{t}^{\top}(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})\varepsilon_{t}]+O_{p}(T^{-1/2}).

(C.34)

The claim of the lemma follows from Assumption
2
(i), and
(
C.27
)-(
C.34
).
Q.E.D.

Lemma C.5
.

Under Assumptions
2
(i, ii) and
3
, we
have:

T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{S,t}y_{t}-{\mathbb{E}[}y_{S,t}y_{t}])=O_{p}(1).

Proof of Lemma
C.5
.
Since
\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}S_{t}=1
, from (
C.25
) we have

y_{S,t}=\frac{1}{1-\phi\psi}S_{t}^{\top}(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})+\frac{\phi}{1-\phi\psi}\varepsilon_{t}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle y_{S,t}y_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\phi\psi}\left(\mathbf{I}_{n}+\frac{\phi\psi}{1-\phi\psi}\mathbf{1}_{n}S_{t}^{\top}\right)(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})^{\top}S_{t}

\displaystyle+\frac{\phi}{1-\phi\psi}\left(\mathbf{I}_{n}+\frac{\phi\psi}{1-\phi\psi}\mathbf{1}_{n}S_{t}^{\top}\right)(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})\varepsilon_{t}

\displaystyle+\frac{\phi}{(1-\phi\psi)^{2}}S_{t}^{\top}(u_{t}+\lambda\eta_{t})\varepsilon_{t}\mathbf{1}_{n}+\frac{\phi^{2}}{(1-\phi\psi)^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{n}\varepsilon_{t}^{2}.

The claim of the lemma thus follows from (
C.30
)-(
C.34
).
Q.E.D.

The GIV estimator depends on
\hat{A}\equiv Q_{-1}\hat{A}_{0}
, which serves
as an estimator of
Q_{-1}A_{0}
. Since
A_{0}
collects the eigenvectors
associated with the smallest
n-\bar{r}
eigenvalues of
S_{y}
, it is not
uniquely identified when
n-\bar{r}>1
. Therefore, it is generally unrealistic
to expect that
\hat{A}_{0}
(and hence
\hat{A}
) converges to
A_{0}
as

T\rightarrow\infty
. Instead, the lemma below shows that a suitably rotated
version of
\hat{A}_{0}
is a consistent estimator of
A_{0}
.

Lemma C.6
.

Define
K_{0}\equiv(A_{0,\bot}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0})(A_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0})^{-1}
and

\hat{H}_{0}\equiv\left(\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}+K_{0}^{\top}K_{0}\right)^{-1/2}(A_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}+K_{0}^{\top}A_{0,\bot}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}).

(C.35)

Under Assumptions
2
and
3
, we have

\hat{A}_{0}\hat{H}_{0}^{\top}-A_{0}=A_{0,\bot}(\bar{\sigma}_{u}^{2}\mathbf{I}_{\bar{r}-1}-\Lambda_{\bot})^{-1}A_{0,\bot}^{\top}\hat{\Delta}_{y}A_{0}+O_{p}(T^{-1}),

(C.36)

where
\hat{\Delta}_{y}\equiv\hat{S}_{y}-S_{y}
. Moreover,
\hat{H}_{0}

satisfies
\hat{H}_{0}^{\top}\hat{H}_{0}=\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}
wpa1.

Proof of Lemma
C.6
.
The proof follows directly
from Lemmas
C.7
-
C.10
.
Q.E.D.

Lemma C.7
.

Under Assumptions
2
and
3
, we have

||\hat{A}_{0}^{\top}A_{0}||_{o}\geq 1-O_{p}(T^{-1/2})\text{ \ \ and
\ \ }\left\|K_{0}\right\|=O_{p}(T^{-1/2}),

(C.37)

where
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{o}
denotes the matrix operator norm.

Proof of Lemma
C.7
.
By Lemma
C.4
,

\hat{\Delta}_{y}\equiv\hat{S}_{y}-S_{y}=O_{p}(T^{-1/2}).

(C.38)

Recall that
\hat{A}_{0}
collects the orthonormal eigenvectors associated
with the smallest
n-\bar{r}
eigenvalues of
\hat{S}_{y}
. We next apply the
Davis–Kahan
\sin\Theta
theorem by verifying the conditions of Theorem 2 in

Yu, Wang, and Samworth 2015
. Specifically, we aim to establish that

\big\lVert A_{0,\bot}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}\big\rVert\leq\frac{2\min((n-\bar{r})^{1/2}||\hat{\Delta}_{y}||_{o},||\hat{\Delta}_{y}||)}{\mu_{n-\bar{r}+1}-\bar{\sigma}_{u}^{2}}.

(C.39)

Let
\Theta(A_{0},\hat{A}_{0})
be the
(n-\bar{r})\times(n-\bar{r})
diagonal
matrix of principal angles between the true and estimated eigenspaces, with

j
-th diagonal entry
\arccos(\sigma_{j})
, where
\sigma_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\sigma_{n-\bar{r}}
are the singular values of
A_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}
.
Define
\sin(\Theta(A_{0},\hat{A}_{0}))
componentwise. Set the parameters of
Theorem 2 in
Yu, Wang, and Samworth 2015
as

p=n-1\text{, \ \ }r=\bar{r}\text{, \ }s=n-1\text{ \ and \ \ }d=s-r+1=n-\bar{r}.

Let
\tilde{\mu}_{j}\equiv\mu_{n-j}
for
j=1,\ldots,n-1
, and
\tilde{\mu}_{n}=-\infty
, such that
\tilde{\mu}_{j}\geq\tilde{\mu}_{j+1}
. By
Assumption
2
(iii),

\min\{\tilde{\mu}_{r-1}-\tilde{\mu}_{r},\tilde{\mu}_{s}-\tilde{\mu}_{s+1}\}=\min\{\tilde{\mu}_{\bar{r}-1}-\tilde{\mu}_{\bar{r}},\tilde{\mu}_{n-1}-\tilde{\mu}_{n}\}=\mu_{n-\bar{r}+1}-\mu_{n-\bar{r}}=\mu_{n-\bar{r}+1}-\bar{\sigma}_{u}^{2}>K^{-1}.

(C.40)

Since
A_{0}
and
\hat{A}_{0}
consist of the eigenvectors corresponding to
the smallest
n-\bar{r}
eigenvalues of
S_{y}
and
\hat{S}_{y}
,
respectively, Theorem 2 of
Yu, Wang, and Samworth 2015
yields

\big\lVert\sin(\Theta(A_{0},\hat{A}_{0}))\big\rVert\leq\frac{2\min\left\{d^{1/2}||\hat{\Delta}_{y}||_{o},||\hat{\Delta}_{y}||\right\}}{\mu_{n-\bar{r}+1}-\bar{\sigma}_{u}^{2}}.

(C.41)

Finally, because
||\sin(\Theta(A_{0},\hat{A}_{0}))||=||A_{0,\bot}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}||
(see the expression below (A4) in
Yu, Wang, and Samworth 2015
), the
desired bound in (
C.39
) follows directly from
(
C.41
).

Using (
C.39
) along with (
C.38
) and Assumption

2
(iii), we obtain

\big\lVert A_{0,\bot}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}\big\rVert=O_{p}(T^{-1/2}).

(C.42)

Let
P_{0}\equiv(A_{0},A_{0,\bot})
. Since
P_{0}
and
\hat{A}_{0}
are
orthonormal by construction,

(P_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0})^{\top}(P_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0})=\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}.

Expanding the left-hand side,

\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}=((A_{0},A_{0,\bot})^{\top}\hat{A}_{0})^{\top}(A_{0},A_{0,\bot})^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}=\hat{A}_{0}^{\top}A_{0}A_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}+\hat{A}_{0}^{\top}A_{0,\bot}A_{0,\bot}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}.

(C.43)

Taking operator norms and applying (
C.42
),

\big\lVert\hat{A}_{0}^{\top}A_{0}\big\rVert_{o}^{2}\geq 1-\big\lVert\hat{A}_{0}^{\top}A_{0,\bot}\big\rVert_{o}^{2}\geq 1-\big\lVert\hat{A}_{0}^{\top}A_{0,\bot}\big\rVert^{2}=1-O_{p}(T^{-1}).

(C.44)

This proves the first claim of (
C.37
).

From (
C.43
) and (
C.44
),
\hat{A}_{0}^{\top}A_{0}
is nonsingular wpa1.
Hence
K_{0}\equiv(A_{0,\bot}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0})(A_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0})^{-1}
is well defined wpa1. This, together with (
C.42
) and
(
C.44
) shows

\left\|K_{0}\right\|=O_{p}(T^{-1/2}),

which establishes the second claim.
Q.E.D.

Lemma C.8
.

Under Assumptions
2
and
3
, we have wpa1,

\hat{A}_{0}\hat{H}_{0}^{\top}=P_{0}\left(\begin{array}[c]{c}\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}\\
K_{0}\end{array}\right)(\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}+K_{0}^{\top}K_{0})^{-1/2}\text{ \ and \ }\hat{H}_{0}^{\top}\hat{H}_{0}=\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}},

(C.45)

where
P_{0}\equiv(A_{0},A_{0,\bot})
.

Proof of Lemma
C.8
.
Under Assumption

2
, we can use (
C.42
) and (
C.43
) in
the proof of Lemma
C.7
to show that

\left\|\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}-\hat{A}_{0}^{\top}A_{0}A_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}\right\|=O_{p}(T^{-1}),

(C.46)

which implies that
A_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}
is invertible wpa1. Conditioning
on the event that
A_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}
is invertible, we may decompose

P_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}=(A_{0},A_{0,\bot})^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}
as

P_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}=\left(\begin{array}[c]{c}A_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}\\
A_{0,\bot}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[c]{c}\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}\\
(A_{0,\bot}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0})(A_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0})^{-1}\end{array}\right)A_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}=\left(\begin{array}[c]{c}\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}\\
K_{0}\end{array}\right)A_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}.

(C.47)

Because both
P_{0}
and
\hat{A}_{0}
have orthonormal columns,

(P_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0})^{\top}(P_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0})=\hat{A}_{0}^{\top}P_{0}P_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}=\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}},

so
P_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}
itself has orthonormal columns. Define

U_{0}\equiv\left(\begin{array}[c]{c}\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}\\
K_{0}\end{array}\right)(\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}+K_{0}^{\top}K_{0})^{-1/2}.

(C.48)

Then
U_{0}^{\top}U_{0}=\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}
, so
U_{0}
also has
orthonormal columns.

Since
A_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}
and
\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}+K_{0}^{\top}K_{0}

are nonsingular, (
C.47
) and (
C.48
) imply that

\mathrm{\operatorname{col}}(P_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0})=\mathrm{\operatorname{col}}(U_{0})\text{, \ wpa1.}

(C.49)

Hence there exists a square matrix
R_{0}\
such that

P_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}=U_{0}R_{0}.

(C.50)

Because both
P_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}
and
U_{0}
have orthonormal columns,
and
R_{0}
is a square matrix, we have

R_{0}^{\top}R_{0}=R_{0}R_{0}^{\top}=\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}.

(C.51)

It follows that:

(P_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0})(P_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0})^{\top}=(U_{0}R_{0})(U_{0}R_{0})^{\top}=U_{0}R_{0}R_{0}^{\top}U_{0}^{\top}=U_{0}U_{0}^{\top}.

(C.52)

By definition of
\hat{H}_{0}
(see Lemma
C.6
),

\hat{H}_{0}=U_{0}^{\top}(P_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}),

(C.53)

based on which we obtain

\hat{A}_{0}\hat{H}_{0}^{\top}=\hat{A}_{0}\hat{A}_{0}^{\top}P_{0}U_{0}=P_{0}(P_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0})(P_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0})^{\top}U_{0}=P_{0}U_{0}U_{0}^{\top}U_{0}=P_{0}U_{0},

(C.54)

where we use
P_{0}P_{0}^{\top}=I_{n-1}
, (
C.52
) and

U_{0}^{\top}U_{0}=\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}
. This proves the first claim of the lemma.

For the second claim,

\hat{H}_{0}^{\top}\hat{H}_{0}=(P_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0})^{\top}U_{0}U_{0}^{\top}(P_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0})=(U_{0}R_{0})^{\top}U_{0}U_{0}^{\top}(U_{0}R_{0})=R_{0}^{\top}(U_{0}^{\top}U_{0})^{2}R_{0}=\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}},

using
U_{0}^{\top}U_{0}=\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}
, (
C.50
),
(
C.51
) and (
C.53
). Both statements in the lemma
now follow.
Q.E.D.

Lemma C.9
.

Under Assumptions
2
and
3
, we have

\tilde{A}_{0}-A_{0}=A_{0,\bot}K_{0}+O_{p}(T^{-1}),

(C.55)

where

\tilde{A}_{0}\equiv P_{0}\left(\begin{array}[c]{c}\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}\\
K_{0}\end{array}\right)(\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}+K_{0}^{\top}K_{0})^{-1/2}.

(C.56)

Proof of Lemma
C.9
.
The proof uses
K_{0}=(A_{0,\bot}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0})(A_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0})^{-1}
, which holds
wpa1 by (
C.46
) in the proof of Lemma
C.8
. From the
definition of
\tilde{A}_{0}
,

\displaystyle\tilde{A}_{0}-A_{0}

\displaystyle=(A_{0}+A_{0,\bot}K_{0})(\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}+K_{0}^{\top}K_{0})^{-1/2}-A_{0}

\displaystyle=A_{0,\bot}K_{0}+\left(A_{0}+A_{0,\bot}K_{0}\right)\left(\left(\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}+K_{0}^{\top}K_{0}\right)^{-1/2}-\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}\right).

(C.57)

By the perturbation inequality for positive definite matrices (see, e.g.,
Lemma A.2 in
Belloni, Chernozhukov, Chetverikov, and Kato 2015
, applied with
A=\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}+K_{0}^{\top}K_{0}
and
B=\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}
in that lemma),

\big\lVert(\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}+K_{0}^{\top}K_{0})^{1/2}-\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}\big\rVert_{o}\leq||K_{0}^{\top}K_{0}||_{o}.

(C.58)

Since
||K_{0}^{\top}K_{0}||_{o}=O_{p}(T^{-1})
by Lemma
C.7
, we
obtain

\big\lVert(\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}+K_{0}^{\top}K_{0})^{1/2}-\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}\big\rVert_{o}\leq||K_{0}^{\top}K_{0}||_{o}=O_{p}(T^{-1}).

(C.59)

Next, Lemma
C.7
along with the triangle inequality and
(
C.59
) implies

\displaystyle||(A_{0}+A_{0,\bot}K_{0})(\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}+K_{0}^{\top}K_{0})^{-1/2}||_{o}\leq(\rho_{\max}((\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}+K_{0}^{\top}K_{0})^{-1}))^{1/2}||A_{0}+A_{0,\bot}K_{0}||_{o}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|A_{0}\right\|_{o}+\left\|A_{0,\bot}\right\|_{o}\left\|K_{0}\right\|_{o}=1+O_{p}(T^{-1/2})\leq 2,

(C.60)

wpa1. Combining (
C.59
) and (
C.60
) yields

\displaystyle\big\lVert(A_{0}+A_{0,\bot}K_{0})((\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}+K_{0}^{\top}K_{0})^{-1/2}-\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}})\big\rVert_{o}

\displaystyle\leq\big\lVert(A_{0}+A_{0,\bot}K_{0})(\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}+K_{0}^{\top}K_{0})^{-1/2}\big\rVert_{o}\big\lVert(\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}+K_{0}^{\top}K_{0})^{1/2}-\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}\big\rVert_{o}=O_{p}(T^{-1}).

(C.61)

The statement of the lemma follows from (
C.57
) and
(
C.61
).
Q.E.D.

Lemma C.10
.

Under Assumptions
2
and
3
, we have

K_{0}=(\bar{\sigma}_{u}^{2}\mathbf{I}_{\bar{r}-1}-\Lambda_{\bot})^{-1}A_{0,\bot}^{\top}\hat{\Delta}_{y}A_{0}+O_{p}(T^{-1}).

Proof of Lemma
C.10
.
The proof uses
(A_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0})^{-}=(A_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0})^{-1}
, which holds wpa1 by
(
C.46
) in the proof of Lemma
C.8
. Let
\hat{\Lambda}

be the diagonal matrix of the
n-\bar{r}
smallest eigenvalues of
\hat{S}_{y}
. By definition of
\hat{A}_{0}
,
\hat{S}_{y}\hat{A}_{0}=\hat{A}_{0}\hat{\Lambda}
. Premultiplying by
P_{0}^{\top}
and using
\hat{S}_{y}=S_{y}+\hat{\Delta}_{y}

P_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}\hat{\Lambda}=P_{0}^{\top}\hat{S}_{y}\hat{A}_{0}=P_{0}^{\top}\hat{S}_{y}P_{0}P_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}=(P_{0}^{\top}S_{y}P_{0}+P_{0}^{\top}\hat{\Delta}_{y}P_{0})P_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}.

(C.62)

Since

P_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}=\left(\begin{array}[c]{c}A_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}\\
A_{0,\bot}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}\end{array}\right)\text{, }P_{0}^{\top}S_{y}P_{0}=\left(\begin{array}[c]{cc}\bar{\sigma}_{u}^{2}\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}&0\\
0&\Lambda_{\bot}\end{array}\right)\text{ and }P_{0}^{\top}\hat{\Delta}_{y}P_{0}=\left(\begin{array}[c]{cc}\hat{\Delta}_{y,11}&\hat{\Delta}_{y,12}\\
\hat{\Delta}_{y,21}&\hat{\Delta}_{y,22}\end{array}\right),

where
\hat{\Delta}_{y,11}\equiv A_{0}^{\top}\hat{\Delta}_{y}A_{0}
,

\hat{\Delta}_{y,12}\equiv A_{0}^{\top}\hat{\Delta}_{y}A_{0,\bot}
,

\hat{\Delta}_{y,22}\equiv A_{0,\bot}^{\top}\hat{\Delta}_{y}A_{0,\bot}
and

\hat{\Delta}_{y,21}^{\top}=\hat{\Delta}_{y,12}
, equation (
C.62
) can be written as

\left(\begin{array}[c]{c}A_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}\hat{\Lambda}\\
A_{0,\bot}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}\hat{\Lambda}\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[c]{c}(\bar{\sigma}_{u}^{2}\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}+\hat{\Delta}_{y,11})A_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}+\hat{\Delta}_{y,12}A_{0,\bot}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}\\
\hat{\Delta}_{y,21}A_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}+(\Lambda_{\bot}+\hat{\Delta}_{y,22})A_{0,\bot}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}\end{array}\right).

(C.63)

The top block of (
C.63
) implies that

A_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}\hat{\Lambda}=(\bar{\sigma}_{u}^{2}\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}+\hat{\Delta}_{y,11})A_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}+\hat{\Delta}_{y,12}A_{0,\bot}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}.

Premultiplying by
(A_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0})^{-1}
, we obtain

\hat{\Lambda}=(A_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0})^{-1}(\bar{\sigma}_{u}^{2}\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}+\hat{\Delta}_{y,11})A_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}+(A_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0})^{-1}\hat{\Delta}_{y,12}A_{0,\bot}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}.

(C.64)

The second block of (
C.63
) implies that

A_{0,\bot}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}\hat{\Lambda}=\hat{\Delta}_{y,21}A_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}+(\Lambda_{\bot}+\hat{\Delta}_{y,22})A_{0,\bot}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0}.

Using (
C.64
) and
K_{0}=(A_{0,\bot}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0})(A_{0}^{\top}\hat{A}_{0})^{-1}
, we obtain

K_{0}(\bar{\sigma}_{u}^{2}\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}+\hat{\Delta}_{y,11})+K_{0}\hat{\Delta}_{y,12}K_{0}=\hat{\Delta}_{y,21}+(\Lambda_{\bot}+\hat{\Delta}_{y,22})K_{0}.

Rearranging,

(\bar{\sigma}_{u}^{2}\mathbf{I}_{\bar{r}-1}-\Lambda_{\bot})K_{0}=\hat{\Delta}_{y,21}-K_{0}\hat{\Delta}_{y,11}+\hat{\Delta}_{y,22}K_{0}-K_{0}\hat{\Delta}_{y,12}K_{0}.

(C.65)

From (
C.38
) and Lemma
C.7
, we have

\big\lVert K_{0}\hat{\Delta}_{y,11}\big\rVert=\big\lVert K_{0}A_{0}^{\top}\hat{\Delta}_{y}A_{0}\big\rVert\leq\left\|K_{0}\right\|\left\|A_{0}\right\|^{2}\big\rVert\hat{\Delta}_{y}\big\rVert=O_{p}(T^{-1}).

(C.66)

Similarly,

\big\lVert\hat{\Delta}_{y,22}K_{0}\big\rVert=\big\lVert A_{0,\bot}^{\top}\hat{\Delta}_{y}A_{0,\bot}K_{0}\big\rVert\leq\left\|K_{0}\right\|\left\|A_{0,\bot}\right\|^{2}\big\rVert\hat{\Delta}_{y}\big\rVert=O_{p}(T^{-1}),

(C.67)

and

\big\lVert K_{0}\hat{\Delta}_{y,12}K_{0}\big\rVert=\big\lVert K_{0}A_{0}^{\top}\hat{\Delta}_{y}A_{0,\bot}K_{0}\big\rVert\leq\left\|K_{0}\right\|^{2}\left\|A_{0,\bot}\right\|\left\|A_{0}\right\|\big\rVert\hat{\Delta}_{y}\big\rVert=O_{p}(T^{-3/2}).

(C.68)

Substituting (
C.66
)-(
C.68
) into (
C.65
),

(\bar{\sigma}_{u}^{2}\mathbf{I}_{\bar{r}-1}-\Lambda_{\bot})K_{0}=\hat{\Delta}_{y,21}+O_{p}(T^{-1}),

which together with Assumption
2
(iii) establishes the
claim.
Q.E.D.

Lemma C.11
.

Under Assumptions
1
,
2
and
3
, we
have

T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}{\tilde{A}^{\top}}y_{t}b_{t}=A^{\top}T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}\xi_{b,t}+O_{p}(T^{-1/2})\text{, \ for }b\in\{v,\varepsilon\},

(C.69)

where
\tilde{A}\equiv Q_{-1}\tilde{A}_{0}
and
\tilde{A}_{0}=\hat{A}_{0}\hat{H}_{0}^{\top}
by (
C.45
) and (
C.56
).

Proof of Lemma
C.11
.
Under Assumptions

2
(i, ii) and
3
, we can apply Lemmas
C.4
and
C.5
to
obtain

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}v_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}])=O_{p}(T^{-1/2})\quad\text{and}\quad T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}\varepsilon_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}\varepsilon_{t}])=O_{p}(T^{-1/2}).

(C.70)

From Lemma
C.6
, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{A}-A

\displaystyle=Q_{-1}(\hat{A}_{0}\hat{H}_{0}^{\top}-A_{0})

\displaystyle=Q_{-1}A_{0,\bot}(\overline{\sigma}_{u}^{2}\mathbf{I}_{\bar{r}-1}-\Lambda_{\bot})^{-1}A_{0,\bot}^{\top}\hat{\Delta}_{y}A_{0}+O_{p}(T^{-1})

\displaystyle=Q_{-1}A_{0,\bot}(\overline{\sigma}_{u}^{2}\mathbf{I}_{\bar{r}-1}-\Lambda_{\bot})^{-1}A_{0,\bot}^{\top}Q_{-1}^{\top}\left(T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}])\right)Q_{-1}A_{0}+O_{p}(T^{-1})

\displaystyle=\Upsilon\,T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}])A+O_{p}(T^{-1})=O_{p}(T^{-1/2}),

(C.71)

where the last equality follows from Assumption
2
(i, iii, iv)
and Lemma
C.4
. Combining (
C.70
) and (
C.71
),
we obtain

\displaystyle T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}\tilde{A}^{\top}y_{t}v_{t}

\displaystyle=T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}A^{\top}y_{t}v_{t}+T^{1/2}(\tilde{A}-A)^{\top}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{t}v_{t}

\displaystyle=T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}A^{\top}y_{t}v_{t}+T^{1/2}(\tilde{A}-A)^{\top}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}]+O_{p}(T^{-1/2})

\displaystyle=A^{\top}T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{t}v_{t}

\displaystyle\quad+A^{\top}T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}])\,\Upsilon\,T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}]+O_{p}(T^{-1/2}).

(C.72)

Next, note that
A\equiv Q_{-1}A_{0}
is orthogonal to
(\mathbf{1}_{n},\lambda)
. Using (
19
), we can write

A^{\top}y_{t}=A_{0}^{\top}Q_{-1}^{\top}(\phi p_{t}\mathbf{1}_{n}+\lambda\eta_{t}+u_{t})=A_{0}^{\top}Q_{-1}^{\top}u_{t}.

Together with the definition of
v_{t}
, this implies

A^{\top}\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}]=A_{0}^{\top}Q_{-1}^{\top}\mathbb{E}[u_{t}v_{t}]=A_{0}^{\top}Q_{-1}^{\top}\mathbb{E}[u_{t}(y_{e,t}-\phi p_{t})].

(C.73)

Since
y_{e,t}=e^{\top}y_{t}
and
y_{t}=\phi p_{t}\mathbf{1}_{n}+\lambda\eta_{t}+u_{t}
, we have

\mathbb{E}[u_{t}(y_{e,t}-\phi p_{t})]=\mathbb{E}[u_{t}(\eta_{t}^{\top}\lambda^{\top}+u_{t}^{\top})]e.

By Assumption
1
, it follows that

Q_{-1}^{\top}\mathbb{E}[(u_{t}\eta_{t}^{\top}\lambda^{\top}+u_{t}u_{t}^{\top})]e=n^{-1}Q_{-1}^{\top}(\mathbf{1}_{n}\Gamma_{\eta u,t}^{\top}\lambda^{\top}+\sigma_{u,t}^{2}\mathbf{I}_{n})\mathbf{1}_{n}=\mathbf{0}_{n-1}.

(C.74)

Combining (
C.73
) and (
C.74
) yields

A^{\top}\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}]=0.

(C.75)

Substituting this into (
C.72
) and using the definition of

\xi_{v,t}
establishes (
C.69
) for
b=v
. The proof for

b=\varepsilon
follows by analogous arguments.
Q.E.D.

Appendix D
Proof of Consistency of the Variance Estimator

In this section, we establish the consistency of the variance matrix estimator

\hat{V}
proposed in the algorithm under the assumption that
\xi_{t}
is
uncorrelated across
t
. The following conditions are useful for proving this result.

Assumption D.1
.

(i) For any two distinct eigenvalues
\mu_{j_{1}}
and

\mu_{j_{2}}
of
S_{y}
,
\left|\mu_{j_{1}}-\mu_{j_{2}}\right|>K^{-1}
; (ii) for any sequence
\{c_{t}\}_{t\leq T}\subset\mathbb{R}
with

\max_{t\leq T}|c_{t}|\leq K
, and
a,b\in\{u,\eta,\varepsilon\}
,

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}c_{t}\big(a_{t}b_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[a_{t}b_{t}^{\top}]\big)=o_{p}(1);

(iii) for every random variable of the form
b_{t}=\prod_{j=1}^{4}z_{j,t}
,

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(b_{t}-\mathbb{E}[b_{t}])=o_{p}(1),

where each
z_{j,t}
belongs to
\{u_{i,t},\eta_{k,t},\varepsilon_{t},S_{t}^{\top}u_{t},s_{i,t}\eta_{k,t}:i\leq n,k\leq r\}
; (iv)
\max_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[(u_{t}^{\top}u_{t})^{2}+\varepsilon_{t}^{4}+(\eta_{t}^{\top}\eta_{t})^{2}]\leq K
.

Assumption
D.1
(i) imposes a uniform separation (gap) between
distinct eigenvalues of
S_{y}
, which is useful for establishing the
consistency of
\hat{\Upsilon}
used in constructing
\hat{\xi}_{b,t}
for

b\in\{v,\varepsilon\}
. Since
\mu_{1}=\ldots=\mu_{n-\bar{r}}=\bar{\sigma}_{u}^{2}<\mu_{n-\bar{r}+1}
, it is clear that Assumption
2
(iii) is implied by Assumption
D.1
(i). Assumption

D.1
(ii) is analogous to Assumption
2
(ii) and can
be verified using a law of large numbers. Assumption
D.1
(iii)
imposes a law of large numbers for fourth-order products formed from

\varepsilon_{t}
and components of
u_{t}
and
\eta_{t}
. Finally,
Assumption
D.1
(iv) imposes uniform fourth-moment bounds on the
shocks in the demand and supply equations.

Theorem D.1
.

Suppose that
\xi_{t}
is uncorrelated across
t
. Under
Assumptions
1
,
2
,
3
,
4
(i, ii, iii),
and
D.1
, we have

\hat{V}=V+o_{p}(1),

where
\hat{V}
is defined in (
A.2
).

Proof of Theorem
D.1
.
Let
\gamma\equiv\Upsilon\big(T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}]\big)
. We then have

\displaystyle\xi_{v,t}

\displaystyle\equiv y_{t}v_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}]+(y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}])\Upsilon\Big(T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}]\Big)

\displaystyle=y_{t}v_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}]+(y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}])\gamma

\displaystyle=y_{t}v_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}]+(y_{\,\gamma,t}y_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{\,\gamma,t}y_{t}]),

which implies that

\xi_{v,t}^{\top}\xi_{v,t}\leq K\Big(v_{t}^{2}y_{t}^{\top}y_{t}+y_{\gamma,t}^{2}y_{t}^{\top}y_{t}+\|\mathbb{E}[v_{t}y_{t}]\|^{2}+\|\mathbb{E}[y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}]\|^{2}\Big).

(D.1)

Under Assumption
2
(i), we can apply (
B.11
) to
show that

\max_{t\leq T}\left\|\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}]\right\|\leq\max_{t\leq T}||\mathbb{E}[y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}]||+\left|\phi\right|\max_{t\leq T}\left\|\mathbb{E}[y_{t}p_{t}]\right\|\leq K.

(D.2)

This together with (
D.19
), the triangle
inequality and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality implies

\left\|\gamma\right\|\leq K

(D.3)

Note that
v_{t}=y_{e,t}-\phi p_{t}
. By (
C.25
) and
(
C.26
), both
y_{t}
and
p_{t}
are linear functions of
u_{t}
,

\eta_{t}
, and
\varepsilon_{t}
. It then follows from Assumptions

2
(i),
3
(ii) and
D.1
(iv) that

\max_{t\leq T}\Big(\mathbb{E}[v_{t}^{2}y_{t}^{\top}y_{t}]+\mathbb{E}[y_{\gamma,t}^{2}y_{t}^{\top}y_{t}]\Big)\leq K.

(D.4)

Combining (
D.1
) and (
D.4
) with Markov’s inequality
yields

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\xi_{v,t}^{\top}\xi_{v,t}=O_{p}(1).

(D.5)

Similarly, we can show that
T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\xi_{\varepsilon,t}^{\top}\xi_{\varepsilon,t}=O_{p}(1)
. Together with (
D.5
), this implies

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\xi_{t}^{\top}\xi_{t}=O_{p}(1).

(D.6)

By the triangle inequality, the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, Lemma
D.8
,
and (
D.6
), we have

\big\lVert T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\hat{\xi}_{t}-\xi_{t})(\hat{\xi}_{t}-\xi_{t})^{\top}\big\rVert\leq T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\|\hat{\xi}_{t}-\xi_{t}\|^{2}=o_{p}(1),

and

\big\lVert T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\hat{\xi}_{t}-\xi_{t})\xi_{t}^{\top}\big\rVert\leq T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\|\hat{\xi}_{t}-\xi_{t}\|\,\|\xi_{t}\|=o_{p}(1),

which, along with Lemma
D.7
, implies

\displaystyle T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\hat{\xi}_{t}\hat{\xi}_{t}^{\top}-T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[\xi_{t}\xi_{t}^{\top}]

\displaystyle=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\xi_{t}\xi_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[\xi_{t}\xi_{t}^{\top}])+T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\hat{\xi}_{t}-\xi_{t})(\hat{\xi}_{t}-\xi_{t})^{\top}

\displaystyle\quad+T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\hat{\xi}_{t}-\xi_{t})\xi_{t}^{\top}+T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\xi_{t}(\hat{\xi}_{t}-\xi_{t})^{\top}

\displaystyle=o_{p}(1).

(D.7)

Since
\xi_{t}
is uncorrelated across
t
, we have
V=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[\xi_{t}\xi_{t}^{\top}]
. Thus, the result follows from
(
D.7
) once we show that

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\hat{\xi}_{t}=o_{p}(1).

(D.8)

To verify (
D.8
), note that by the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\big\lVert T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\hat{\xi}_{t}\big\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\big\lVert T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\hat{\xi}_{t}-\xi_{t})\big\rVert+\big\lVert T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\xi_{t}\big\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\Big(T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\|\hat{\xi}_{t}-\xi_{t}\|^{2}\Big)^{1/2}+O_{p}(T^{-1/2})=o_{p}(1),

where the second inequality follows from the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and
Assumption
4
(i), and the last equality follows from Lemma

D.8
. This completes the proof.
Q.E.D.

D.1
Useful Lemmas for consistency of variance estimator

Lemma D.1
.

Suppose that the eigenvalues
\{\mu_{j}\}_{n-\bar{r}+1\leq j\leq n-1}
take
m
distinct values
\{\mu_{j}^{\ast}\}_{1\leq j\leq m}
, ordered increasingly, with multiplicities
\{s_{j}\}_{1\leq j\leq m}
. That is,

\mu_{n-\bar{r}+\bar{s}_{j-1}+1}=\cdots=\mu_{n-\bar{r}+\bar{s}_{j}}=\mu_{j}^{\ast},

(D.9)

for
j\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
, where
\bar{s}_{j-1}=\sum_{l=1}^{j-1}s_{l}
. Let

A_{j}
collect the orthonormal eigenvectors of
S_{y}
associated with the
eigenvalues
\{\mu_{n-\bar{r}+\bar{s}_{j-1}+l}\}_{l=1}^{s_{j}}
, and let

\hat{A}_{j}
collect the orthonormal eigenvectors of
\hat{S}_{y}
associated
with
\{\hat{\mu}_{n-\bar{r}+\bar{s}_{j-1}+l}\}_{l=1}^{s_{j}}
. Then under
Assumptions
2
(i, ii),
3
,
4
(i, ii), and

D.1
(i), there exists an orthogonal matrix
\hat{O}_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{j}\times s_{j}}
such that for each
j\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
,

\big\lVert\hat{A}_{j}\hat{O}_{j}-A_{j}\big\lVert=O_{p}(T^{-1/2}).

Proof of Lemma
D.1
.
Fix
j\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
,
and define

r_{j}\equiv n-\bar{r}+\bar{s}_{j-1}+1,\qquad s_{j}^{\ast}\equiv n-\bar{r}+\bar{s}_{j}.

Then
r_{j}\leq s_{j}^{\ast}
, and by (
D.9
), the columns of

A_{j}
and
\hat{A}_{j}
are the orthonormal eigenvectors of
S_{y}
and

\hat{S}_{y}
, respectively, associated with the block of eigenvalues

\mu_{r_{j}}=\cdots=\mu_{s_{j}^{\ast}}=\mu_{j}^{\ast}.

Since Theorem 2 of
Yu, Wang, and Samworth 2015
is stated for eigenvalues ordered in
decreasing order, we apply it to
-S_{y}
and
-\hat{S}_{y}
. By
construction,
\mu_{r_{1}-1}=\mu_{n-\bar{r}}=\bar{\sigma}_{u}^{2}
. Following

Yu, Wang, and Samworth 2015
, we set
\mu_{n}=\infty
, which implies
\mu_{s_{j}^{\ast}+1}=\infty
. It then follows that there exists an orthogonal matrix
\hat{O}_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{j}\times s_{j}}
such that

\big\lVert\hat{A}_{j}\hat{O}_{j}-A_{j}\big\lVert\leq\frac{2^{3/2}\min\left\{s_{j}^{1/2}\big\lVert\hat{S}_{y}-S_{y}\big\lVert_{o},\,\big\lVert\hat{S}_{y}-S_{y}\big\lVert\right\}}{\min\left\{\mu_{r_{j}}-\mu_{r_{j}-1},\,\text{\ }\mu_{s_{j}^{\ast}+1}-\mu_{s_{j}^{\ast}}\right\}}.

(D.10)

We next show that the denominator in (
D.10
) is bounded away
from zero uniformly in
j
. For
j\in\{2,\ldots,m\}
, the eigenvalues

\mu_{r_{j}-1}
and
\mu_{r_{j}}
are distinct, and hence Assumption

D.1
(i) implies

\mu_{r_{j}}-\mu_{r_{j}-1}\geq K^{-1}.

Similarly, for
j\in\{1,\ldots,m-1\}
, the eigenvalues
\mu_{s_{j}^{\ast}}

and
\mu_{s_{j}^{\ast}+1}
are distinct, so Assumption
D.1
(i)
implies

\mu_{s_{j}^{\ast}+1}-\mu_{s_{j}^{\ast}}\geq K^{-1}.

For the boundary block
j=1
, the left gap is

\mu_{r_{1}}-\mu_{r_{1}-1}=\mu_{n-\bar{r}+1}-\mu_{n-\bar{r}},

where
\mu_{n-\bar{r}}=\bar{\sigma}_{u}^{2}
is not contained in the
collection
\{\mu_{j}\}_{n-\bar{r}+1\leq j\leq n-1}
. By Assumption

D.1
(i), this gap is bounded away from zero. Therefore,

\min\{\mu_{r_{j}}-\mu_{r_{j}-1},\,\mu_{s_{j}^{\ast}+1}-\mu_{s_{j}^{\ast}}\}\geq K^{-1}

(D.11)

for all
j\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
.

Finally, by (
B.57
),
\|\hat{S}_{y}-S_{y}\|=O_{p}(T^{-1/2})
. Combining this with (
D.10
) and
(
D.11
) establishes the claim of the lemma.
Q.E.D.

Lemma D.2
.

Under Assumptions
2
(i, ii, iii, iv),
3
,

4
(i, ii) and
D.1
(i), we have

\hat{\Upsilon}=\Upsilon+O_{p}(T^{-1/2})=O_{p}(1),

(D.12)

where
\hat{\Upsilon}\equiv Q_{-1}\hat{A}_{0,\bot}(\hat{\sigma}_{u}^{2}\mathbf{I}_{\bar{r}-1}-\hat{\Lambda}_{\bot})^{-1}\hat{A}_{0,\bot}^{\top}Q_{-1}^{\top}
and
\Upsilon\equiv Q_{-1}A_{0,\bot}(\bar{\sigma}_{u}^{2}\mathbf{I}_{\bar{r}-1}-\Lambda_{\bot})^{-1}A_{0,\bot}^{\top}Q_{-1}^{\top}
.

Proof of Lemma
D.2
.
Suppose that the eigenvalues

\{\mu_{j}\}_{n-\bar{r}+1\leq j\leq n-1}
take
m
distinct values
\{\mu_{j}^{\ast}\}_{1\leq j\leq m}
, with multiplicities
\{s_{j}\}_{1\leq j\leq m}
. Then we can write

A_{0,\bot}=(A_{1},\ldots,A_{m})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\hat{A}_{0,\bot}=(\hat{A}_{1},\ldots,\hat{A}_{m}),

where
A_{j}
and
\hat{A}_{j}
collect the orthonormal eigenvectors of

S_{y}
and
\hat{S}_{y}
, respectively, associated with the eigenvalue

\mu_{j}^{\ast}
of multiplicity
s_{j}
. By Lemma
D.1
, there
exists a block diagonal matrix
\hat{H}_{0,\bot}^{\top}=\mathrm{diag}(\hat{O}_{1},\ldots,\hat{O}_{m})
, where each
\hat{O}_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{j}\times s_{j}}
is orthogonal, such that

\hat{A}_{0,\bot}\hat{H}_{0,\bot}^{\top}=A_{0,\bot}+O_{p}(T^{-1/2})=O_{p}(1).

(D.13)

From
\|\hat{S}_{y}-S_{y}\|=O_{p}(T^{-1/2})
, we have

\hat{\sigma}_{u}^{2}=\bar{\sigma}_{u}^{2}+O_{p}(T^{-1/2}),\qquad\hat{\Lambda}_{\bot}=\Lambda_{\bot}+O_{p}(T^{-1/2}),

(D.14)

where
\bar{\sigma}_{u}^{2}
and
\Lambda_{\bot}
are bounded by
(
B.11
). Since

\Lambda_{\bot}=\mathrm{diag}(\mu_{1}^{\ast}I_{s_{1}},\ldots,\mu_{m}^{\ast}I_{s_{m}}),

and
\hat{H}_{0,\bot}^{\top}
is block diagonal with orthogonal blocks, we
have

\hat{H}_{0,\bot}\Lambda_{\bot}\hat{H}_{0,\bot}^{\top}=\Lambda_{\bot}.

(D.15)

Therefore,

(\hat{\sigma}_{u}^{2}I_{\bar{r}-1}-\hat{H}_{0,\bot}\hat{\Lambda}_{\bot}\hat{H}_{0,\bot}^{\top})-(\bar{\sigma}_{u}^{2}I_{\bar{r}-1}-\Lambda_{\bot})=(\hat{\sigma}_{u}^{2}-\bar{\sigma}_{u}^{2})I_{\bar{r}-1}-\hat{H}_{0,\bot}(\hat{\Lambda}_{\bot}-\Lambda_{\bot})\hat{H}_{0,\bot}^{\top}=O_{p}(T^{-1/2}).

(D.16)

By Assumption
D.1
(i), the eigenvalues of
\Lambda_{\bot}-\bar{\sigma}_{u}^{2}I_{\bar{r}-1}
are bounded below by
K^{-1}
. Hence, by
(
D.16
),

\rho_{\min}\bigl(\hat{H}_{0,\bot}\hat{\Lambda}_{\bot}\hat{H}_{0,\bot}^{\top}-\hat{\sigma}_{u}^{2}I_{\bar{r}-1}\bigr)\geq(2K)^{-1},\qquad\text{wpa1.}

(D.17)

Since
\hat{H}_{0,\bot}^{\top}\hat{H}_{0,\bot}=I_{\bar{r}-1}
, we have

(\hat{\sigma}_{u}^{2}I_{\bar{r}-1}-\hat{\Lambda}_{\bot})^{-1}=\hat{H}_{0,\bot}^{\top}(\hat{\sigma}_{u}^{2}I_{\bar{r}-1}-\hat{H}_{0,\bot}\hat{\Lambda}_{\bot}\hat{H}_{0,\bot}^{\top})^{-1}\hat{H}_{0,\bot}.

Notice that

\displaystyle\hat{\Upsilon}-\Upsilon

\displaystyle=Q_{-1}\hat{A}_{0,\bot}\hat{H}_{0,\bot}^{\top}(\hat{\sigma}_{u}^{2}I_{\bar{r}-1}-\hat{H}_{0,\bot}\hat{\Lambda}_{\bot}\hat{H}_{0,\bot}^{\top})^{-1}\hat{H}_{0,\bot}\hat{A}_{0,\bot}^{\top}Q_{-1}^{\top}

\displaystyle\quad-Q_{-1}A_{0,\bot}(\bar{\sigma}_{u}^{2}I_{\bar{r}-1}-\Lambda_{\bot})^{-1}A_{0,\bot}^{\top}Q_{-1}^{\top}

\displaystyle=Q_{-1}(\hat{A}_{0,\bot}\hat{H}_{0,\bot}^{\top}-A_{0,\bot})(\hat{\sigma}_{u}^{2}I_{\bar{r}-1}-\hat{H}_{0,\bot}\hat{\Lambda}_{\bot}\hat{H}_{0,\bot}^{\top})^{-1}\hat{H}_{0,\bot}\hat{A}_{0,\bot}^{\top}Q_{-1}^{\top}

\displaystyle\quad+Q_{-1}A_{0,\bot}\Bigl[(\hat{\sigma}_{u}^{2}I_{\bar{r}-1}-\hat{H}_{0,\bot}\hat{\Lambda}_{\bot}\hat{H}_{0,\bot}^{\top})^{-1}-(\bar{\sigma}_{u}^{2}I_{\bar{r}-1}-\Lambda_{\bot})^{-1}\Bigr]\hat{H}_{0,\bot}\hat{A}_{0,\bot}^{\top}Q_{-1}^{\top}

\displaystyle\quad+Q_{-1}A_{0,\bot}(\bar{\sigma}_{u}^{2}I_{\bar{r}-1}-\Lambda_{\bot})^{-1}(\hat{H}_{0,\bot}\hat{A}_{0,\bot}^{\top}-A_{0,\bot}^{\top})Q_{-1}^{\top}.

By (
D.13
)–(
D.17
), each term on the right-hand
side is
O_{p}(T^{-1/2})
. Therefore,

\hat{\Upsilon}-\Upsilon=O_{p}(T^{-1/2}),

(D.18)

which establishes the first equality in (
D.12
).

Since
Q_{-1}^{\top}Q_{-1}=\mathbf{I}_{n-1}
,
A_{0,\bot}^{\top}A_{0,\bot}=\mathbf{I}_{\bar{r}-1}
and
\Lambda_{\bot}\equiv\mathrm{diag}((\mu_{j})_{n-\bar{r}+1\leq j\leq n-1})
, from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and
Assumption
2
(iii), it follows that

\left\|\Upsilon\right\|=\big\lVert Q_{-1}A_{0,\bot}(\bar{\sigma}_{u}^{2}\mathbf{I}_{\bar{r}-1}-\Lambda_{\bot})^{-1}A_{0,\bot}^{\top}Q_{-1}^{\top}\big\lVert\leq\frac{\left\|Q_{-1}\right\|^{2}\left\|A_{0,\bot}\right\|^{2}(\bar{r}-1)}{\mu_{n-\bar{r}+1}-\bar{\sigma}_{u}^{2}}\leq K,

(D.19)

which together with (
D.18
) shows the second equality
in (
D.12
).
Q.E.D.

Lemma D.3
.

Under Assumptions
1
,
2
,
3
and

4
(i, iii), we have for
b\in\{v,\varepsilon\}

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{t}\hat{b}_{t}=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{t}b_{t}]+O_{p}(T^{-1/2})=O_{p}(1).

Proof of Lemma
D.3
.
Because
v_{t}\equiv y_{e,t}-\phi p_{t}
, we can write

\displaystyle T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{t}v_{t}

\displaystyle=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}]+T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\bigl(y_{t}v_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}]\bigr)

\displaystyle=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}]+T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\bigl(y_{t}y_{e,t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}y_{e,t}]\bigr)-\phi\,T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\bigl(y_{t}p_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}p_{t}]\bigr)

\displaystyle=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}]+O_{p}(T^{-1/2})=O_{p}(1),

(D.20)

where the third equality follows from Lemma
C.4
, and the final
equality uses (
D.2
).

Since
\hat{\theta}_{0}(\hat{A})
denotes the GIV estimator with weighting
matrix
W_{0,T}=\mathbf{I}_{2n}
(see Step 2 of the Implementation Algorithm 1
in Appendix
A
), Assumption
4
(ii) holds with

W_{0}=\mathbf{I}_{2n}
. Therefore, under Assumptions
1
,

2
,
3
, and
4
(i, iii), we can apply
(
42
) of Theorem
1
to obtain

\hat{\theta}_{0}(\hat{A})-\theta=O_{p}(T^{-1/2}).

(D.21)

Combining this with (
B.12
) yields

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{t}\hat{v}_{t}-T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{t}v_{t}=-\bigl(\hat{\phi}_{0}(\hat{A})-\phi\bigr)T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{t}p_{t}=O_{p}(T^{-1/2}).

(D.22)

The result for
b=v
follows from (
D.20
) and (
D.22
). The
case
b=\varepsilon
can be established analogously and is therefore
omitted.
Q.E.D.

Lemma D.4
.

Under Assumptions
2
(i),
3
and

D.1
(ii), we have for any
c_{t}\in\mathbb{R}
with
\max_{t\leq T}|c_{t}|\leq K
,

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}c_{t}(y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-{\mathbb{E}[}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}])=o_{p}(1)\text{ \ \ \ and \ \ \ }T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}c_{t}(p_{t}y_{t}-{\mathbb{E}[}p_{t}y_{t}])=o_{p}(1).

Proof of Lemma
D.4
.
Using the reduced-form
representations of
y_{t}
and
p_{t}
in (
C.25
) and
(
C.26
), the stated results follow by the same argument as in the
proof of Lemma
C.4
, with Assumption
2
(ii) replaced by
Assumption
D.1
(ii).
Q.E.D.

Lemma D.5
.

Under Assumptions
2
(i),
3
(ii) and

D.1
(iii, iv), we have

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{a_{1},t}y_{a_{2},t}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{a_{1},t}y_{a_{2},t}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}])=o_{p}(1),

for any
a_{1},a_{2}\in\{a\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:

\left\|a\right\|\leq K\}
.

Proof of Lemma
D.5
.
From the reduced form
expression of
y_{t}
in (
C.25
), the triangle inequality, and the
Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle\left\|y_{t}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\frac{|\phi\psi|n^{1/2}\left\|S_{t}\right\|}{|1-\phi\psi|}\right)(\left\|u_{t}\right\|+\left\|\lambda\right\|\left\|\eta_{t}\right\|)+\frac{|\phi|n^{1/2}\left|\varepsilon_{t}\right|}{|1-\phi\psi|}

\displaystyle\leq K(\left\|u_{t}\right\|+\left\|\eta_{t}\right\|+\left|\varepsilon_{t}\right|)

where the second inequality follows by Assumptions
2
(i) and

3
(ii). Therefore, by Assumption
D.1
(iv),

\mathbb{E}[\left\|y_{t}\right\|^{4}]\leq K(\mathbb{E}[\left\|u_{t}\right\|^{4}+\left\|\eta_{t}\right\|^{4}+\varepsilon_{t}^{4}])\leq K.

This together with the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality implies that

\big\lVert\mathbb{E}[y_{a_{1},t}y_{a_{2},t}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}]\big\lVert\leq K,

for any
a_{1},a_{2}\in\{a\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:

\left\|a\right\|\leq K\}
. Since
y_{t}
is a linear function of
u_{t}
,
\eta_{t}
,
\varepsilon_{t}
,
S_{t}^{\top}u_{t}
and
(\eta_{t}^{\top}\otimes S_{t}^{\top})\mathrm{vec}(\lambda)
, each entry of
y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}
is a finite
linear combination of random variables of the form

b_{1,t}=\prod_{j=1}^{2}z_{j,t},

where each
z_{j,t}
belongs to
\{u_{i,t},\eta_{k,t},\varepsilon_{t},S_{t}^{\top}u_{t},(\eta_{t}^{\top}\otimes S_{t}^{\top})\mathrm{vec}(\lambda):i\leq n,k\leq r\}
, with coefficients that do not depend on
t
.
Since

y_{a_{1},t}y_{a_{2},t}=a_{1}^{\top}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}a_{2},

and
\|a_{1}\|,\|a_{2}\|\leq K
, the scalar
y_{a_{1},t}y_{a_{2},t}
is also a finite linear combination of terms of the form

w_{1,t}
, with coefficients bounded uniformly over admissible
a_{1},a_{2}
.
Therefore, for each
(i_{1},i_{2})
, the
(i_{1},i_{2})
th entry of

y_{a_{1},t}y_{a_{2},t}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}

is a finite linear combination of random variables of the form

b_{t}=\prod_{j=1}^{4}z_{j,t}

with coefficients uniformly bounded over admissible
a_{1},a_{2}
, where each

z_{j,t}
belongs to the same set above. Assumption
D.1
(iii)
then yields

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\bigl(b_{t}-\mathbb{E}[b_{t}]\bigr)=o_{p}(1)

for each such fourth-order polynomial. Since
n
is fixed, each entry of

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\bigl(y_{a_{1},t}y_{a_{2},t}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{a_{1},t}y_{a_{2},t}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}]\bigr)

is a finite linear combination of
o_{p}(1)
terms, and is therefore

o_{p}(1)
. This proves the claim.
Q.E.D.

Lemma D.6
.

Under Assumptions
2
(i),
3
(ii) and

D.1
(iii, iv), we have for any
a\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
with
\|a\|\leq K

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\bigl(y_{a,t}p_{t}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{a,t}p_{t}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}]\bigr)=o_{p}(1),

and

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\bigl(p_{t}^{2}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[p_{t}^{2}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}]\bigr)=o_{p}(1).

Proof of Lemma
D.6
.
Under Assumption

2
(i), it follows from (
C.26
) that
p_{t}
is a
linear function of
S_{t}^{\top}u_{t}
,
\eta_{t}\otimes S_{t}
, and

\varepsilon_{t}
. The stated results then follow by the same argument as in
the proof of Lemma
D.5
.
Q.E.D.

Lemma D.7
.

Under Assumptions
2
(i),
3
(ii) and

D.1
(ii, iii, iv), we have

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\xi_{t}\xi_{t}^{\top}=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[\xi_{t}\xi_{t}^{\top}]+o_{p}(1).

Proof of Lemma
D.7
.
By the definition of
\xi_{t}
,
it suffices to show that

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\xi_{b,t}\xi_{b,t}^{\top}=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[\xi_{b,t}\xi_{b,t}^{\top}]+o_{p}(1),

(D.23)

for
b\in\{v,\varepsilon\}
, and

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\xi_{v,t}\xi_{\varepsilon,t}^{\top}=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[\xi_{v,t}\xi_{\varepsilon,t}^{\top}]+o_{p}(1).

(D.24)

We establish (
D.23
) for
b=v
; the remaining cases follow analogously.

Recall that
\gamma\equiv\Upsilon\big(T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}]\big)
, and

\displaystyle\xi_{v,t}

\displaystyle\equiv y_{t}v_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}]+(y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}])\,\Upsilon\Big(T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}]\Big)

\displaystyle=y_{t}v_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}]+(y_{\,\gamma,t}y_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{\,\gamma,t}y_{t}]).

This implies that

\displaystyle T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\xi_{v,t}\xi_{v,t}^{\top}

\displaystyle=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(v_{t}y_{t}-\mathbb{E}[v_{t}y_{t}])(v_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[v_{t}y_{t}^{\top}])

\displaystyle\quad+T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(v_{t}y_{t}-\mathbb{E}[v_{t}y_{t}])(y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}^{\top}])

\displaystyle\quad+T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}])(v_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[v_{t}y_{t}^{\top}])

\displaystyle\quad+T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}])(y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}^{\top}]).

(D.25)

We next study the four terms on the right-hand side of (
D.25
) one by one.

Step 1: The first term.
Expanding the first term
yields

\displaystyle T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}v_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}])(y_{t}v_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}])^{\top}

\displaystyle=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}v_{t}^{2}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}+T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[v_{t}y_{t}]\mathbb{E}[v_{t}y_{t}^{\top}]-T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\bigl(\mathbb{E}[v_{t}y_{t}]v_{t}y_{t}^{\top}+v_{t}y_{t}\mathbb{E}[v_{t}y_{t}^{\top}]\bigr).

(D.26)

Since
v_{t}=y_{e,t}-\phi p_{t}
, Lemmas
D.5
and
D.6
imply
that

\displaystyle T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(v_{t}^{2}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[v_{t}^{2}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}])

\displaystyle=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{e,t}^{2}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{e,t}^{2}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}])

\displaystyle\quad+\phi^{2}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(p_{t}^{2}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[p_{t}^{2}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}])

\displaystyle\quad-2\phi T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{e,t}p_{t}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{e,t}p_{t}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}])

\displaystyle=o_{p}(1).

(D.27)

Moreover, since
\max_{t\leq T}\|\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}]\|\leq K
by
(
D.2
), Lemma
D.4
implies that

\displaystyle T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}](y_{t}^{\top}v_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}^{\top}v_{t}])

\displaystyle=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}](y_{t}^{\top}y_{e,t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}^{\top}y_{e,t}])

\displaystyle\quad-\phi T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}](y_{t}^{\top}p_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}^{\top}p_{t}])

\displaystyle=o_{p}(1).

(D.28)

Combining (
D.26
)–(
D.28
) yields

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}v_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}])(y_{t}v_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}])^{\top}=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[(y_{t}v_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}])(y_{t}v_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}])^{\top}]+o_{p}(1).

(D.29)

Step 2: The cross terms.
For the second term on the
right-hand side of (
D.25
), we have

\displaystyle T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}v_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}])(y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}^{\top}])

\displaystyle=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{\gamma,t}v_{t}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{t}v_{t}\mathbb{E}[y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}^{\top}]

\displaystyle\quad-T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}]y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}^{\top}+T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}]\mathbb{E}[y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}^{\top}].

(D.30)

Using (
D.3
), Lemmas
D.5
and
D.6
, we obtain

\displaystyle T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{\gamma,t}v_{t}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{\gamma,t}v_{t}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}])

\displaystyle=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{\gamma,t}y_{e,t}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{\gamma,t}y_{e,t}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}])

\displaystyle-\phi T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{\gamma,t}p_{t}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{\gamma,t}p_{t}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}])\overset{}{=}o_{p}(1).

(D.31)

Moreover, from (
B.11
) and (
D.3
),

\max_{t\leq T}\|\mathbb{E}[y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}^{\top}]\|\leq K,

(D.32)

which, together with Lemma
D.4
, implies

\displaystyle T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}v_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}])\mathbb{E}[y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}^{\top}]

\displaystyle=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}y_{e,t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}y_{e,t}])\mathbb{E}[y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}^{\top}]

\displaystyle-\phi T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}p_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}p_{t}])\mathbb{E}[y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}^{\top}]\overset{}{=}o_{p}(1).

(D.33)

Similarly, we can show that

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}](y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}^{\top}])=o_{p}(1).

(D.34)

From (
D.30
)-(
D.34
), we conclude that

\displaystyle T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}v_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}])(y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}^{\top}])\,

\displaystyle=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}\left[(y_{t}v_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}])(y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}^{\top}])\right]+o_{p}(1).\,\,

(D.35)

The third term in (
D.25
) can be handled analogously.

Step 3: The last term.
Finally, for the fourth term
in (
D.25
), we have

\displaystyle T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}])(y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}^{\top}])

\displaystyle=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{\gamma,t}^{2}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}]y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}^{\top}

\displaystyle-T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}\mathbb{E}[y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}^{\top}]+T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}]\mathbb{E}[y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}^{\top}]

\displaystyle=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{\gamma,t}^{2}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}]-T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}]\mathbb{E}[y_{\gamma,t}y_{t}^{\top}]+o_{p}(1)

(D.36)

where the second equality holds by (
D.32
), Lemma
D.4
and
Lemma
D.5
.

Combining (
D.25
), (
D.29
), (
D.35
), and
(
D.36
) establishes (
D.23
) for
b=v
, completing the
proof.
Q.E.D.

Lemma D.8
.

Under Assumptions
1
,
2
,
3
,

4
(i, iii) and
D.1
, we have

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\hat{\xi}_{t}-\xi_{t})^{\top}(\hat{\xi}_{t}-\xi_{t})=o_{p}(1).

Proof of Lemma
D.8
.
Let
\hat{\gamma}\equiv\hat{\Upsilon}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{t}\hat{v}_{t}
. Then, by Lemma

D.2
and Lemma
D.3
,

\hat{\gamma}-\gamma=(\hat{\Upsilon}-\Upsilon)T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{t}\hat{v}_{t}+\Upsilon\Big(T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{t}\hat{v}_{t}-T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}]\Big)=o_{p}(1).

(D.37)

Because

\displaystyle\hat{\xi}_{v,t}

\displaystyle\equiv y_{t}\hat{v}_{t}-T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{t}\hat{v}_{t}+\Big(y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}\Big)\hat{\Upsilon}\Big(T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{t}\hat{v}_{t}\Big)

\displaystyle=y_{t}\hat{v}_{t}-T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{t}\hat{v}_{t}+\Big(y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}\Big)\hat{\gamma}

and

\xi_{v,t}=y_{t}v_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}]+(y_{\,\gamma,t}y_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{\,\gamma,t}y_{t}]),

we have

\hat{\xi}_{v,t}-\xi_{v,t}=y_{t}(\hat{v}_{t}-v_{t})+\left(y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}]\right)(\hat{\gamma}-\gamma)-\Delta_{T},

(D.38)

where

\Delta_{T}=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}\hat{v}_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}])+T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}])\hat{\gamma}.

Therefore by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\hat{\xi}_{v,t}-\xi_{v,t})^{\top}(\hat{\xi}_{v,t}-\xi_{v,t})

\displaystyle\leq 3T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\hat{v}_{t}-v_{t})^{2}y_{t}^{\top}y_{t}+3||\Delta_{T}||^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+3(\hat{\gamma}-\gamma)^{\top}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\left(y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}]\right)^{2}(\hat{\gamma}-\gamma).

(D.39)

Since
\hat{v}_{t}-v_{t}=-(\hat{\phi}_{0}(\hat{A})-\phi)p_{t}
, we have

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\hat{v}_{t}-v_{t})^{2}y_{t}^{\top}y_{t}=(\hat{\phi}_{0}(\hat{A})-\phi)^{2}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}p_{t}^{2}y_{t}^{\top}y_{t}.

(D.40)

Under Assumptions
2
(i) and
D.1
(iv), together with
the reduced-form expressions for
y_{t}
and
p_{t}
in (
C.25
) and
(
C.26
), we have

\max_{t\leq T}\left(\mathbb{E}[p_{t}^{2}y_{t}^{\top}y_{t}]+\mathbb{E}[(y_{t}^{\top}y_{t})^{2}]\right)\leq K.

(D.41)

Hence, by Markov’s inequality,

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}p_{t}^{2}y_{t}^{\top}y_{t}=O_{p}(1),

(D.42)

which, together with (
D.21
) and (
D.40
), implies

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\hat{v}_{t}-v_{t})^{2}y_{t}^{\top}y_{t}=o_{p}(1).

(D.43)

From Lemma
C.4
, Lemma
D.3
, (
D.3
), and
(
D.37
), it follows that

||\Delta_{T}||\leq\Big\lVert T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}\hat{v}_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}v_{t}])\Big\lVert+\Big\lVert T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}])\hat{\gamma}\Big\lVert=o_{p}(1).

(D.44)

Finally, by (
D.41
), the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and Markov’s
inequality,

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\left(y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}]\right)^{2}=O_{p}(1),

which, together with (
D.37
), implies

(\hat{\gamma}-\gamma)^{\top}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\left(y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}y_{t}^{\top}]\right)^{2}(\hat{\gamma}-\gamma)=o_{p}(1).

(D.45)

Combining (
D.39
), (
D.43
), (
D.44
), and
(
D.45
) yields

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\hat{\xi}_{v,t}-\xi_{v,t})^{\top}(\hat{\xi}_{v,t}-\xi_{v,t})=o_{p}(1).

Similarly, we can show that

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\hat{\xi}_{\varepsilon,t}-\xi_{\varepsilon,t})^{\top}(\hat{\xi}_{\varepsilon,t}-\xi_{\varepsilon,t})=o_{p}(1).

Combining these results establishes the claim of the lemma.
Q.E.D.

Appendix E
Asymptotic Variance with Exogenous Regressors

This section studies the asymptotic variance of the GIV estimator proposed in
Subsection
4.2
, where both the supply and demand equations
include exogenous regressors. The key challenge is to account for the role of
the estimation error in
\beta
in the asymptotic variance of the GIV
estimator. As we show below, this estimation error is asymptotically
negligible and can therefore be ignored. Consequently, the standard errors of
the GIV estimator, as well as the
J
-test, can be constructed as if
\beta

were known. We begin by introducing a set of conditions that characterize the
properties of the additional regressors in the model.

Assumption E.1
.

(i) For any
t\leq T
and
i\leq n
, we have

\mathbb{E}[x_{i,t}(u_{t}^{\top},\eta_{t}^{\top})]=\mathbf{0}_{d_{x}\times(n+r)}
,
\mathbb{E}[x_{i,t}\varepsilon_{t}]=\mathbf{0}_{d_{x}}
and
\mathbb{E}[w_{t}\varepsilon_{t}]=\mathbf{0}_{d_{w}\times 1}
; (ii) let
\tilde{\epsilon}_{t}\equiv\tilde{\lambda}\eta_{t}+\tilde{u}_{t}
, then

T^{1/2}(\hat{\beta}-\beta)=\Bigl(T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[\tilde{x}_{t}^{\top}\tilde{x}_{t}]\Bigr)^{-1}\Bigl(T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}\tilde{x}_{t}^{\top}\tilde{\epsilon}_{t}\Bigr)+o_{p}(1),

where
\ \rho_{\min}(T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[\tilde{x}_{t}^{\top}\tilde{x}_{t}])\geq K^{-1}
and
T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}\tilde{x}_{t}^{\top}\tilde{\epsilon}_{t}=O_{p}(1)
; (iii) for
a\in\{u,\eta,\varepsilon\}
,

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\big(x_{t}\otimes a_{t}-\mathbb{E}[x_{t}\otimes a_{t}]\big)=O_{p}(T^{-1/2});

(iv)
\max_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[||x_{t}||^{2}+||w_{t}||^{2}]\leq K
,

\left\|\beta\right\|\leq K
and
\left\|\gamma\right\|\leq K
.

Assumption
E.1
(i) ensures that
x_{t}
and
w_{t}
are exogenous
in the demand and supply equations, respectively. Assumption
E.1
(ii) provides a linear representation of the estimation error in
\hat{\beta}
, implying that
\hat{\beta}
is
T^{1/2}
-consistent. Assumption

E.1
(iii) imposes a
T^{-1/2}
law of large numbers for products
of
x_{t}
and the demand and supply shocks. Assumption
E.1
(iv)
places uniform bounds on the second moments of the exogenous regressors, as
well as on the magnitudes of their coefficients.

Since the matrix
\hat{A}
in the moment conditions
\bar{g}_{T}(\theta;\hat{A},\hat{\beta})
in (
62
) plays the same role as in
\bar{g}_{T}(\theta;\hat{A})
defined in (
36
), we can apply the same
arguments as in the proof of Lemma
C.3
to show that both the GIV
estimator
\hat{\theta}(\hat{A})
in (
63
) and the
J
-statistic

T\,\bar{g}_{T}(\hat{\theta}(\hat{A});\hat{A},\hat{\beta})^{\top}W_{0,T}(\hat{A})\bar{g}_{T}(\hat{\theta}(\hat{A});\hat{A},\hat{\beta})

are invariant to nonsingular rotations of
\hat{A}
of the form
\hat{A}C_{1}
, for any nonsingular
(n-\bar{r})\times(n-\bar{r})
matrix
C_{1}
.

Moreover, Lemmas
C.4
(with
y_{t}
in that lemma corresponding to

y_{t}^{\ast}
here) and
E.2
imply that

Q_{-1}^{\top}(\hat{\Sigma}_{\hat{y}^{\ast}}-\bar{\Sigma}_{y^{\ast}})Q_{-1}=O_{p}(T^{-1/2}).

Therefore, we can use the same arguments as in the proof of Lemma

C.6
to obtain

\hat{A}_{0}\hat{H}_{0}^{\top}-A_{0}=A_{0,\bot}(\bar{\sigma}_{u}^{2}\mathbf{I}_{\bar{r}-1}-\Lambda_{\bot})^{-1}A_{0,\bot}^{\top}Q_{-1}^{\top}(\hat{\Sigma}_{\hat{y}^{\ast}}-\bar{\Sigma}_{y^{\ast}})Q_{-1}A_{0}+O_{p}(T^{-1}),

(E.1)

where
\hat{H}_{0}
is nonsingular with
\hat{H}_{0}^{\top}\hat{H}_{0}=\mathbf{I}_{n-\bar{r}}
wpa1. Here
A_{0}
,
A_{0,\bot}
,
\Lambda_{\bot}
, and
\hat{H}_{0}
are defined by replacing
\bar{\Sigma}_{y}
and

\hat{\Sigma}_{y}
in their original definitions with
\bar{\Sigma}_{y^{\ast}}
and
\hat{\Sigma}_{\hat{y}^{\ast}}
, respectively.

Since the contribution of the estimation error in
\beta
to the asymptotic
variance of the GIV estimator arises through
\bar{g}_{T}(\theta;\hat{A},\hat{\beta})
, and given the invariance of both the GIV estimator

\hat{\theta}(\hat{A})
in (
63
) and the
J
-statistic, it suffices
to study the asymptotic variance of
\bar{g}_{T}(\theta;\tilde{A},\hat{\beta})
in order to assess the effect of the estimation error in
\beta
. The
asymptotic expansion of
\bar{g}_{T}(\theta;\tilde{A},\hat{\beta})
is given
in the following lemma.

Lemma E.1
.

Under Assumptions
1
,
2
,

3
and
E.1
, we have

\bar{g}_{T}(\theta;\tilde{A},\hat{\beta})=\mathrm{diag}(A^{\top},A^{\top},\mathbf{I}_{d_{w}})T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\bar{\xi}_{t}+O_{p}(T^{-1}),

where
\bar{\xi}_{t}\equiv(\bar{\xi}_{v,t}^{\top},\bar{\xi}_{\varepsilon,t}^{\top},w_{t}^{\top}\varepsilon_{t})^{\top}
, and for
b\in\{v,\varepsilon\}
,

\bar{\xi}_{b,t}\equiv y_{t}^{\ast}b_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}^{\ast}b_{t}]+(y_{t}^{\ast}y_{t}^{\ast\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}^{\ast}y_{t}^{\ast\top}])\Upsilon\Bigl(T^{-1}\sum_{s\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{s}^{\ast}b_{s}]\Bigr).

(E.2)

Proof of Lemma
E.1
.
The result follows
immediately from Lemmas
E.2
,
E.3
, and

E.4
, and is therefore omitted.
Q.E.D.

Lemma
E.1
shows that the asymptotic variance of
\hat{\theta}(\hat{A})
is determined by that of
T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}\bar{\xi}_{t}
.
Compared with
\xi_{t}
defined in Assumption
4
(i),
\bar{\xi}_{t}
contains the additional component
w_{t}^{\top}\varepsilon_{t}
, which
arises from the moment conditions used to identify and estimate the
coefficients on
w_{t}
in the supply equation. More importantly,
\bar{\xi}_{b,t}
in (
E.2
) has the same form as
\xi_{b,t}
in
(
41
), with the only difference being that
y
there is replaced by

y^{\ast}
here. Therefore, the estimation error in
\beta
does not affect
the asymptotic variance of
\bar{g}_{T}(\theta;\tilde{A},\hat{\beta})
, and
hence does not affect that of the GIV estimator
\hat{\theta}(\hat{A})
in
(
63
). As a result, the standard errors of
\hat{\theta}(\hat{A})
,
the optimal weight matrix, and the
J
-statistic can be constructed as if

\beta
were known.

Lemma E.2
.

Under Assumptions
1
,
2
,
3
and
E.1
, we have

Q_{-1}^{\top}(\hat{\Sigma}_{\hat{y}^{\ast}}-\bar{\Sigma}_{y^{\ast}})Q_{-1}=Q_{-1}^{\top}T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\big(y_{t}^{\ast}y_{t}^{\ast\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}^{\ast}y_{t}^{\ast\top}]\big)Q_{-1}+O_{p}(T^{-1}).

Proof of Lemma
E.2
.
By the definitions of

\hat{y}_{t}^{\ast}
and
\hat{y}_{e,t}^{\ast}
,

\hat{y}_{t}^{\ast}-y_{t}^{\ast}=-x_{t}(\hat{\beta}-\beta),\qquad\ \hat{y}_{e,t}^{\ast}-y_{e,t}^{\ast}=-x_{e,t}(\hat{\beta}-\beta),

(E.3)

where
x_{e,t}\equiv e^{\top}x_{t}
. It follows that

\displaystyle\hat{\Sigma}_{\hat{y}^{\ast}}-\bar{\Sigma}_{y^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\hat{y}_{t}^{\ast}\hat{y}_{t}^{\ast\top}-T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{t}^{\ast}y_{t}^{\ast\top}]

\displaystyle=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}^{\ast}-x_{t}(\hat{\beta}-\beta))(y_{t}^{\ast}-x_{t}(\hat{\beta}-\beta))^{\top}-T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{t}^{\ast}y_{t}^{\ast\top}]

\displaystyle=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\big(y_{t}^{\ast}y_{t}^{\ast\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}^{\ast}y_{t}^{\ast\top}]\big)-T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{t}^{\ast}(\hat{\beta}-\beta)^{\top}x_{t}^{\top}

\displaystyle\quad-T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}x_{t}(\hat{\beta}-\beta)y_{t}^{\ast\top}+T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}x_{t}(\hat{\beta}-\beta)(\hat{\beta}-\beta)^{\top}x_{t}^{\top}.

(E.4)

From Assumption
E.1
(ii),

\hat{\beta}-\beta=O_{p}(T^{-1/2}).

(E.5)

By the triangle and Cauchy–Schwarz inequalities,

\Big\lVert T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}x_{t}(\hat{\beta}-\beta)(\hat{\beta}-\beta)^{\top}x_{t}^{\top}\Big\lVert\leq\|\hat{\beta}-\beta\|^{2}\,T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\|x_{t}\|^{2}=O_{p}(T^{-1}),

(E.6)

where the last equality follows from Assumption
E.1
(iv) and
(
E.5
).

Let
\lambda_{i}
denote the
i
th row of
\lambda
. By Assumptions

E.1
(i, iii)

\displaystyle T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{i,t}^{\ast}x_{t}

\displaystyle=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(\phi p_{t}+\lambda_{i}\eta_{t}+u_{i,t})x_{t}

\displaystyle=\phi T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}p_{t}x_{t}+T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\lambda_{i}\eta_{t}x_{t}+T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}u_{i,t}x_{t}

\displaystyle=\phi T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}p_{t}x_{t}+O_{p}(T^{-1/2}).

(E.7)

Under Assumptions
2
(i, iv) and
E.1
(iv), the
demand and supply equations (
60
)-(
61
) admit the
following reduced form expressions

\displaystyle y_{t}

\displaystyle=\Bigl(\mathbf{I}_{n}+\frac{\phi\psi}{1-\phi\psi}\mathbf{1}_{n}S_{t}^{\top}\Bigr)(u_{t}+x_{t}\beta+\lambda\eta_{t})+\frac{\phi\mathbf{1}_{n}}{1-\phi\psi}(\varepsilon_{t}+w_{t}^{\top}\gamma),

(E.8)

\displaystyle p_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{\psi}{1-\phi\psi}S_{t}^{\top}(u_{t}+x_{t}\beta+\lambda\eta_{t})+\frac{\varepsilon_{t}+w_{t}^{\top}\gamma}{1-\phi\psi}.

(E.9)

From Assumptions
2
(i, iv) and
E.1
(iv), it follows
that

\mathbb{E}[p_{t}^{2}]\leq K.

Together with Assumption
E.1
(iv), the Cauchy–Schwarz
inequality, and Markov’s inequality, this implies that

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}p_{t}x_{t}=O_{p}(1).

(E.10)

Combining (
E.5
), (
E.7
) and
(
E.10
),

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}x_{t}(\hat{\beta}-\beta)y_{t}^{\ast\top}=\phi\Bigl(T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}p_{t}x_{t}\Bigr)(\hat{\beta}-\beta)\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}+O_{p}(T^{-1}).

(E.11)

Since
\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}Q_{-1}=\mathbf{0}_{n-1}
, it follows that

Q_{-1}^{\top}\Bigl(T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}x_{t}(\hat{\beta}-\beta)y_{t}^{\ast\top}\Bigr)Q_{-1}=O_{p}(T^{-1}).

The same bound holds for its transpose. Combining these bounds with the
quadratic term gives the desired result.
Q.E.D.

Lemma E.3
.

Under Assumptions
1
,
2
,

3
and
E.1
, we have

\displaystyle T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}\tilde{A}^{\top}\hat{y}_{t}^{\ast}(\hat{y}_{e,t}^{\ast}-\phi p_{t})

\displaystyle=A^{\top}T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}^{\ast}v_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}^{\ast}v_{t}])

\displaystyle\quad+A^{\top}\Bigl(T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}^{\ast}y_{t}^{\ast\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}^{\ast}y_{t}^{\ast\top}])\Bigr)\Upsilon\Bigl(T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{t}^{\ast}v_{t}]\Bigr)

\displaystyle\quad+O_{p}(T^{-1/2}).

Proof of Lemma
E.3
.
We begin with the
decomposition

\displaystyle T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}\tilde{A}^{\top}\hat{y}_{t}^{\ast}(\hat{y}_{e,t}^{\ast}-\phi p_{t})

\displaystyle=T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}A^{\top}y_{t}^{\ast}(y_{e,t}^{\ast}-\phi p_{t})+T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}A^{\top}y_{t}^{\ast}(\hat{y}_{e,t}^{\ast}-y_{e,t}^{\ast})

\displaystyle\quad+A^{\top}T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}(\hat{y}_{t}^{\ast}-y_{t}^{\ast})(\hat{y}_{e,t}^{\ast}-\phi p_{t})+(\tilde{A}-A)^{\top}T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}\hat{y}_{t}^{\ast}(\hat{y}_{e,t}^{\ast}-\phi p_{t}).

(E.12)

Since
v_{t}\equiv y_{e,t}^{\ast}-\phi p_{t}
, by (
C.75
) we have

T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}A^{\top}y_{t}^{\ast}(y_{e,t}^{\ast}-\phi p_{t})=T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}A^{\top}y_{t}^{\ast}v_{t}=A^{\top}T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}^{\ast}v_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}^{\ast}v_{t}]).

(E.13)

We next control the last three terms on the right-hand side of
(
E.12
).

Step 1: Second term.
Using
\hat{\beta}-\beta=O_{p}(T^{-1/2})
in (
E.5
) and the same arguments as in
(
E.7
), we have

\displaystyle T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{t}^{\ast}(\hat{y}_{e,t}^{\ast}-y_{e,t}^{\ast})

\displaystyle=-T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{t}^{\ast}x_{e,t}(\hat{\beta}-\beta)

\displaystyle=-\mathbf{1}_{n}\Bigl(\phi T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}p_{t}x_{e,t}\Bigr)T^{1/2}(\hat{\beta}-\beta)+O_{p}(T^{-1/2}).

(E.14)

Since
A^{\top}\mathbf{1}_{n}=A_{0}^{\top}Q_{-1}^{\top}\mathbf{1}_{n}=\mathbf{0}_{n-1}
, it follows that

T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}A^{\top}y_{t}^{\ast}(\hat{y}_{e,t}^{\ast}-y_{e,t}^{\ast})=O_{p}(T^{-1/2}).

(E.15)

Step 2: Third term.
By Assumptions
2
(i) and
E.1
(i, iii),

T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}x_{t}(y_{e,t}^{\ast}-\phi p_{t})=O_{p}(1).

Combining this with
\hat{\beta}-\beta=O_{p}(T^{-1/2})
yields

T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}(\hat{y}_{t}^{\ast}-y_{t}^{\ast})(y_{e,t}^{\ast}-\phi p_{t})=-\,T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}x_{t}(\hat{\beta}-\beta)(y_{e,t}^{\ast}-\phi p_{t})=O_{p}(T^{-1/2}).

(E.16)

Moreover, by the same arguments as in (
E.6
),

T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}x_{t}(\hat{\beta}-\beta)x_{e,t}(\hat{\beta}-\beta)=O_{p}(T^{-1/2}).

Therefore,

\displaystyle T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}(\hat{y}_{t}^{\ast}-y_{t}^{\ast})(\hat{y}_{e,t}^{\ast}-\phi p_{t})

\displaystyle=-T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}x_{t}(\hat{\beta}-\beta)(y_{e,t}^{\ast}-\phi p_{t})

\displaystyle\quad+T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}x_{t}(\hat{\beta}-\beta)x_{e,t}(\hat{\beta}-\beta)=O_{p}(T^{-1/2}).

(E.17)

Hence,

A^{\top}T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}(\hat{y}_{t}^{\ast}-y_{t}^{\ast})(\hat{y}_{e,t}^{\ast}-\phi p_{t})=O_{p}(T^{-1/2}).

(E.18)

Step 3: Fourth term.
To study the last term, we
first use (
E.17
) to obtain

\displaystyle T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}\hat{y}_{t}^{\ast}(\hat{y}_{e,t}^{\ast}-\phi p_{t})

\displaystyle=T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{t}^{\ast}(\hat{y}_{e,t}^{\ast}-\phi p_{t})+T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}(\hat{y}_{t}^{\ast}-y_{t}^{\ast})(\hat{y}_{e,t}^{\ast}-\phi p_{t})

\displaystyle=T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{t}^{\ast}v_{t}+T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{t}^{\ast}(\hat{y}_{e,t}^{\ast}-y_{e,t}^{\ast})+O_{p}(T^{-1/2}),

(E.19)

where
v_{t}\equiv y_{e,t}^{\ast}-\phi p_{t}
. From (
E.10
), we
have

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}p_{t}x_{e,t}=O_{p}(1),

(E.20)

which, together with (
E.5
), implies

\mathbf{1}_{n}\Bigl(\phi T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}p_{t}x_{e,t}\Bigr)T^{1/2}(\hat{\beta}-\beta)=O_{p}(1).

(E.21)

From Lemmas
C.4
and
C.6
(with
y_{t}
in that lemma
corresponding to
y_{t}^{\ast}
here), and
E.2
, we have

T^{1/2}(\tilde{A}-A)=\Upsilon\Bigl(T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}^{\ast}y_{t}^{\ast\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}^{\ast}y_{t}^{\ast\top}])\Bigr)A+O_{p}(T^{-1/2})=O_{p}(1),

(E.22)

where the second equality follows from (
D.19
) and Lemma

C.4
.

Using
\Upsilon\mathbf{1}_{n}=0
, together with (
E.14
) and
(
E.21
), we obtain

(\tilde{A}-A)^{\top}T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{t}^{\ast}(\hat{y}_{e,t}^{\ast}-y_{e,t}^{\ast})=O_{p}(T^{-1/2}).

(E.23)

By (
D.2
) and (
C.70
) (with
y_{t}
there corresponding
to
y_{t}^{\ast}
here), we have

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{t}^{\ast}v_{t}]=O(1),\quad T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}^{\ast}v_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}^{\ast}v_{t}])=O_{p}(1).

(E.24)

From (
E.22
) and (
E.24
), it follows that

\displaystyle(\tilde{A}-A)^{\top}T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{t}^{\ast}v_{t}

\displaystyle=A^{\top}\Bigl(T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}^{\ast}y_{t}^{\ast\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}^{\ast}y_{t}^{\ast\top}])\Bigr)\Upsilon\Bigl(T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{t}^{\ast}v_{t}]\Bigr)

\displaystyle\quad+O_{p}(T^{-1/2}).

(E.25)

Combining (
E.19
), (
E.23
) and
(
E.25
), we obtain

\displaystyle(\tilde{A}-A)^{\top}T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}\hat{y}_{t}^{\ast}(\hat{y}_{e,t}^{\ast}-\phi p_{t})

\displaystyle=A^{\top}\Bigl(T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}^{\ast}y_{t}^{\ast\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}^{\ast}y_{t}^{\ast\top}])\Bigr)\Upsilon\Bigl(T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{t}^{\ast}v_{t}]\Bigr)

\displaystyle\quad+O_{p}(T^{-1/2}).

(E.26)

The result then follows from (
E.12
), (
E.13
), (
E.15
), (
E.18
), and
(
E.26
).
Q.E.D.

Lemma E.4
.

Under Assumptions
1
,
2
,

3
and
E.1
, we have

\displaystyle T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}\tilde{A}^{\top}\hat{y}_{t}^{\ast}\varepsilon_{t}

\displaystyle=A^{\top}T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}^{\ast}\varepsilon_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}^{\ast}\varepsilon_{t}])

\displaystyle\quad+A^{\top}\Bigl(T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}^{\ast}y_{t}^{\ast\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}^{\ast}y_{t}^{\ast\top}])\Bigr)\Upsilon\Bigl(T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{t}^{\ast}\varepsilon_{t}]\Bigr)

\displaystyle+O_{p}(T^{-1/2}).

Proof of Lemma
E.4
.
We begin with the
decomposition

T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}\tilde{A}^{\top}\hat{y}_{t}^{\ast}\varepsilon_{t}=T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}A^{\top}y_{t}^{\ast}\varepsilon_{t}+(\tilde{A}-A)^{\top}T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{t}^{\ast}\varepsilon_{t}+T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}\tilde{A}^{\top}(\hat{y}_{t}^{\ast}-y_{t}^{\ast})\varepsilon_{t}.

(E.27)

By arguments similar to those used to establish (
C.75
), we have

T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}A^{\top}\mathbb{E}[y_{t}^{\ast}\varepsilon_{t}]=\mathbf{0}_{n-\bar{r}}.

Therefore,

T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}A^{\top}y_{t}^{\ast}\varepsilon_{t}=A^{\top}T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}^{\ast}\varepsilon_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}^{\ast}\varepsilon_{t}]).

(E.28)

We next study the last term on the right-hand side of (
E.27
).
By definition,

T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}\tilde{A}^{\top}(\hat{y}_{t}^{\ast}-y_{t}^{\ast})\varepsilon_{t}=-A^{\top}T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}x_{t}\varepsilon_{t}(\hat{\beta}-\beta)-(\tilde{A}-A)^{\top}T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}x_{t}\varepsilon_{t}(\hat{\beta}-\beta),

(E.29)

where we use
\hat{y}_{t}^{\ast}-y_{t}^{\ast}=-x_{t}(\hat{\beta}-\beta)
. From
Assumption
E.1
(iii), together with the condition
T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[x_{t}\varepsilon_{t}]=0
, it follows that

T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}x_{t}\varepsilon_{t}=O_{p}(1).

(E.30)

This, combined with (
E.5
), (
E.22
), and
(
E.29
), implies that

T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}\tilde{A}^{\top}(\hat{y}_{t}^{\ast}-y_{t}^{\ast})\varepsilon_{t}=O_{p}(T^{-1/2}).

(E.31)

Next, by arguments similar to those used in (
E.24
), we have

T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{t}^{\ast}\varepsilon_{t}]=O(1),\qquad T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}^{\ast}\varepsilon_{t}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}^{\ast}\varepsilon_{t}])=O_{p}(1).

Combining this with (
E.22
) yields

(\tilde{A}-A)^{\top}T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}y_{t}^{\ast}\varepsilon_{t}=A^{\top}\Bigl(T^{-1/2}\sum_{t\leq T}(y_{t}^{\ast}y_{t}^{\ast\top}-\mathbb{E}[y_{t}^{\ast}y_{t}^{\ast\top}])\Bigr)\Upsilon\Bigl(T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}\mathbb{E}[y_{t}^{\ast}\varepsilon_{t}]\Bigr)+O_{p}(T^{-1/2}).

(E.32)

Substituting (
E.28
), (
E.31
) and
(
E.32
) into (
E.27
) completes the
proof.
Q.E.D.

Appendix F
Additional Simulation Results

This appendix reports two additional sets of simulation results related to
Section
5
: the power of the feasible
J
-test in the extended
design and the performance of the BIC criterion in estimating the number of
latent factors.

Figure F.1:
Empirical Power of the
J
-test: Extended Design (
d_{x}=3
)

Notes.
This figure plots the empirical rejection probabilities of the
J
-test at the
5\%
nominal significance level in the
extended design with
d_{x}=3
exogenous regressors, based on
10{,}000
Monte
Carlo replications. Each panel corresponds to one of the six configurations

(n,r)
listed in (
74
), and within each panel the three curves
correspond to the sample sizes
T\in\{150,300,450\}
. The horizontal axis
is
\rho
, while the vertical axis reports the
rejection rate. The horizontal dashed line indicates the nominal
5\%
level.
Power is reported for the feasible GIV estimator, which selects
\bar{r}
using
the BIC criterion in (
47
)–(
48
) and is implemented according
to Algorithm 1 in Appendix
A
. In the extended design, the estimator additionally partials out
x_{t}\beta
using OLS.

F.1
Power of the
J
-test in the Extended Design

In the extended design with
d_{x}=3
exogenous regressors, the feasible

J
-test exhibits behavior similar to that in the baseline design shown in
Figure
1
: the power increases monotonically with

\rho
, rises substantially as the sample size
T
increases, and approaches
one by
\rho=0.4
across all configurations (see
Figure
F.1
for details). Partialling out the exogenous
regressors
x_{t}
has only a minor effect on power. Relative to the baseline
design, the power curves in the extended design lie only slightly below their
baseline counterparts at intermediate values of
\rho
, reflecting the
additional estimation error from estimating
\beta
.

This table reports BIC selection diagnostics for the rank estimator

\widehat{r}
, based on
10{,}000

(

) and the extended design with

,

, and

\widehat{r}
defined
in (

)–(

), where
\bar{r}=r+1
denotes the true rank of

(\mathbf{1}_{n},\lambda)
.

F.2
BIC Rank Selection

The feasible GIV estimation and inference procedures determine the rank
\bar{r}
using the BIC estimator proposed in (
47
)–(
48
).
Table
F1
shows that the BIC criterion performs remarkably well in
estimating the true rank across all configurations of
(n,r)
in both the
baseline and extended designs. In particular, the modal value of
\widehat{r}

always coincides with the true rank
\bar{r}=r+1
, and the average value of

\widehat{r}
is extremely close to
\bar{r}
in every case.

The probability of correct selection is uniformly high across all
configurations, typically exceeding
97\%
and often above
99\%
. For
example, in the baseline design with
(n,r)=(5,1)
, the correct selection
probabilities are
0.994
,
0.992
, and
0.993
at
T=150
,
300
, and
450
,
respectively. Even in configurations with a larger number of latent factors,
such as
(n,r)=(10,7)
, the correct selection probabilities remain close to

98\%
in both designs. Moreover, the performance of the BIC criterion is
nearly identical in the baseline and extended designs, indicating that the
additional estimation of
\beta
in the extended model has little effect on
rank determination.

Overall, these results suggest that the proposed BIC procedure provides a
highly reliable estimator of the rank
\bar{r}
, supporting the practical
implementation of the feasible GIV estimation and inference procedures.

Appendix G
Additional Empirical Results

G.1
Variable Construction

We construct the sector-level holding growth
\Delta q_{i,t}
and the
aggregate price change
\Delta p_{t}
following Appendix C of
Gabaix and Koijen 2021
.

The Financial Accounts report the dollar value of each sector’s U.S. equity
holdings, denoted by
w_{i,t}
. Changes in
w_{i,t}
reflect both capital
appreciation of existing holdings and net purchases or sales of equities. To
isolate the latter component, let
q_{i,t}
denote the number of shares held
by sector
i
and let
P_{t}
denote the aggregate equity price. Since

w_{i,t}=q_{i,t}P_{t}
, a sector that does not trade between
t-1
and
t

would end the quarter holding
w_{i,t-1}R_{t}
dollars of equity, where

R_{t}=P_{t}/P_{t-1}
is the gross capital-appreciation return on the
aggregate market. It follows that

\Delta q_{i,t}\equiv\frac{q_{i,t}}{q_{i,t-1}}-1=\frac{w_{i,t}/P_{t}}{w_{i,t-1}/P_{t-1}}-1=\frac{w_{i,t}}{w_{i,t-1}R_{t}}-1,

which is the empirical measure used in (
78
). Define
\Delta f_{i,t}\equiv(w_{i,t}-w_{i,t-1}R_{t})/w_{i,t-1}
. We can equivalently write

\Delta q_{i,t}=\Delta f_{i,t}/R_{t}
, which corresponds to the flow-based
measure reported in Appendix C.1.2 of
Gabaix and Koijen 2021
. The gross
capital-appreciation return
R_{t}
is constructed from the CRSP
value-weighted index excluding dividends and compounded from monthly to
quarterly frequency.

The aggregate price change
\Delta p_{t}
is measured by the quarterly simple
return on the CRSP value-weighted index including dividends, compounded from
monthly returns.

G.2
Sector Construction and Weights

We construct the sector classification from the corporate-equity holdings
reported in Financial Accounts Table L.224, following the data construction in
Appendix C of
Gabaix and Koijen 2021
. The resulting cross-section consists
of twelve equity-holding sectors, listed in Table
G1
. These
sectors correspond closely to the investor categories used in
Gabaix and Koijen 2021
.

For each sector, we compute its time-
t
market share as

S_{i,t}\equiv\frac{w_{i,t-1}}{\sum_{j\leq 12}w_{j,t-1}}.

(G.1)

Thus,
S_{i,t}
measures sector
i
’s share of the total equity holdings of
the twelve modeled sectors and satisfies
\sum_{i=1}^{12}S_{i,t}=1
.
Table
G1
reports the average market share,
\bar{S}_{i}=T^{-1}\sum_{t\leq T}S_{i,t}
, computed over the benchmark sample 1993Q1–2018Q4.

Notes.
The table lists the twelve institutional investor sectors holding U.S. equities in the Financial Accounts of the United States (Table L.224), ranked by average market share over 1993Q1–2018Q4. “Avg. share” denotes the sector’s average share of aggregate equity holdings (in percent), and the shares sum to 100. “Cum. share” denotes the cumulative share accounted for by the sector and all sectors ranked above it. “Cross-section” identifies the estimation samples that include the sector:
n=6
corresponds to the granular core formed by the six largest sectors, while
n=12
corresponds to the full twelve-sector panel.

We rank sectors according to their average market shares
\bar{S}_{i}
. The
six largest sectors jointly account for 97.4% of total equity holdings and
constitute the granular core (
n=6
): households, mutual funds and ETFs, the
foreign sector, private pension funds, state and local pension funds, and life
insurance companies. The remaining six sectors each account for less than
1.2% of total equity holdings and, together with the granular core, form the
full twelve-sector panel (
n=12
).

G.3
Implementation of the Factor-residual IV Estimator

This subsection describes our implementation of the FIV estimator of

Gabaix and Koijen 2021
. To facilitate comparison with their results, we
follow the estimation procedure in Section 4.2 of their paper as closely as possible.

Algorithm 3 (FIV Estimation)

Step 0.
For each sector
i
, let
\hat{\sigma}_{q,i}^{2}

denote the time-series sample variance of
\Delta q_{i,t}
. Construct the
precision weights

E_{i}=\frac{1/\bar{\sigma}_{q,i}^{2}}{\sum_{j\leq n}1/\bar{\sigma}_{q,j}^{2}},\qquad\bar{\sigma}_{q,i}^{2}=\max\bigl\{\hat{\sigma}_{q,i}^{2},\ \operatorname{median}_{j\leq n}\hat{\sigma}_{q,j}^{2}\bigr\}.

Step 1.
Estimate the two-way fixed-effects regression by
weighted least squares:

(\hat{\alpha},\hat{\gamma},\hat{\beta})=\arg\min_{{\alpha_{i}},{\gamma_{t}},{\beta_{i}}}\sum_{t\leq T}\sum_{i\leq n}E_{i}\left(\Delta q_{i,t}-\alpha_{i}-\gamma_{t}-\beta_{i}^{\top}\eta_{o,t}\right)^{2},

where
\alpha_{i}
and
\gamma_{t}
are sector and time fixed effects,
respectively, and
\eta_{o,t}
denotes the vector of observed factors with
sector-specific loadings
\beta_{i}
. The residual is

\Delta\check{q}_{i,t}\equiv\Delta q_{i,t}-\hat{\alpha}_{i}-\hat{\gamma}_{t}-\hat{\beta}_{i}^{\top}\eta_{o,t}.

Step 2.
Extract
r
latent factors, denoted by
\hat{\eta}_{l,t}
, as the principal components of the weighted residuals
E_{i}^{1/2}\Delta\check{q}_{i,t}
. Given the estimated factors
\hat{\eta}_{l,t}
,
estimate the corresponding factor loadings
\hat{\lambda}_{i}
and recover the
idiosyncratic shock
\check{u}_{i,t}
from the regression

\Delta\check{q}_{i,t}=\hat{\lambda}_{i}^{\top}\hat{\eta}_{l,t}+\check{u}_{i,t}.

Step 3.
Construct the FIV as the size-weighted sum of the
estimated idiosyncratic shocks

z_{t}=\sum_{i\leq n}S_{i,t}\,\check{u}_{i,t},

where
S_{i,t}
is defined in (
G.1
), and estimate the market
multiplier from the time-series regression

\Delta p_{t}=\alpha+\kappa z_{t}+\Gamma^{\top}\eta_{t}+e_{t},\text{
\ \ \ }\eta_{t}=(\eta_{o,t}^{\top},\hat{\eta}_{l,t}^{\top})^{\top}

(G.2)

where
\Delta p_{t}
denotes the aggregate market return and
\kappa
is the
aggregate market multiplier.

Step 4.
Recompute the precision weights using the
time-series sample variance of the estimated idiosyncratic shocks
\check{u}_{i,t}
and repeat Steps 1–3 once. Report the coefficient estimates and
their Newey–West standard errors from the time-series regression in
(
G.2
), without adjusting for the estimation error in
z_{t}
and

\eta_{t}
.

In our empirical implementation of the FIV estimator, the observed factors

\eta_{o,t}
consist of GDP growth together with a set of asset-pricing
factors. Specifically, following
Gabaix and Koijen 2021
, we run monthly
cross-sectional regressions of stock returns on log market capitalization, the
log book-to-market ratio, and a momentum signal. Individual stock returns,
prices, and shares outstanding are obtained from CRSP, while book equity used
to construct the book-to-market ratio is obtained from Compustat, matching the
data inputs used by
Gabaix and Koijen 2021
. GDP growth is
computed from real gross domestic product (series GDPC1), produced by the U.S.
Bureau of Economic Analysis and retrieved from the Federal Reserve Economic
Data (FRED) database maintained by the Federal Reserve Bank of St. Louis. The
resulting Fama–MacBeth slope estimates are aggregated to the quarterly
frequency and used as the observed size, value, and momentum factors.
